# EVALUATE.md — MRBD Games & Experiences Quality Playbook


_This is a living document. Update it when you learn what works and what doesn't._

## What Works on Glasses (Validated)

### Proven Patterns
- **Ambient + glanceable:** Things you check for 5 seconds, not 5 minutes (earthquake map, compass, decibel meter)
- **Audio-reactive:** The glasses have a mic right there — experiences that respond to ambient sound feel magical (flow fields, spectrum analyzer)
- **One-hand, zero-attention games:** D-pad games you can play while walking without looking at anything (snake, rhythm tap)
- **Utility you'd actually open daily:** Compass, sound meter, metronome — things with real-world triggers
- **Breathing/meditation:** The always-on HUD is perfect for guided breathing — you don't have to hold anything

### What Makes Glasses Different From Phone
- **Hands-free** — you're doing something else (walking, cooking, waiting)
- **Ambient awareness** — you can see the real world AND the app
- **Mic always available** — audio-reactive is the killer feature
- **Contextual** — compass while hiking, decibel meter at a concert, breathing guide while walking
- **Glanceable** — information at a glance, not deep interaction

## What Fails on Glasses (Learned the Hard Way)

### Anti-Patterns
- **"Pretty animation" with no purpose** — cool for 5 seconds, never opened again
- **Complex interactions** — D-pad has 5 buttons. If your UX needs more, it's wrong.
- **Things that need precision** — pixel art, drawing, anything that needs a cursor
- **Deep reading** — nobody reads paragraphs on a HUD
- **Things phones do better** — calculators, text input, anything with a keyboard
- **Tech demos** — "look, I can render particles!" is not a product

### Red Flags in Proposals
- "Interactive [noun]" where the interaction is just scrolling a list
- Anything that needs a tutorial longer than one sentence
- "Generative art" that's just random pretty colors with no user agency
- Experiences that only work sitting at a desk
- Games that require sustained focus for >2 minutes

## Scoring Rubric (10 dimensions × 5 points = 50 raw, displayed as /100)

### Original 6 Dimensions
| Criterion | 1 (Bad) | 3 (OK) | 5 (Great) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Trigger moment** | Can't think of one | Vaguely useful sometimes | Specific: "I'm at X doing Y" |
| **5-second hook** | Confusing, needs explanation | Makes sense, mildly interesting | Instantly delightful |
| **Glasses advantage** | Phone is better | About equal | Clearly better hands-free |
| **Return visits** | Once and done | Maybe weekly | Daily habit potential |
| **D-pad fit** | Awkward, needs more inputs | Works but clunky | Natural, satisfying |
| **Audio/context use** | Ignores mic and sensors | Uses one sensor | Deeply integrated with environment |

### 4 New Dimensions (added March 2026 to differentiate quality)
| Criterion | 1 (Bad) | 3 (OK) | 5 (Great) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Session Variance** | Identical every time | Some randomization | Deeply procedural with emergent gameplay |
| **Strategic Depth** | Pure reflexes, no decisions | Some tactical choices | Deep resource management and tradeoffs |
| **Surprise / Discovery** | Fully known in 30 seconds | Some unlockables | Genuine emergent discoveries |
| **Craft** | Functional but generic | Well-made | Has a "wow, that's clever" moment |

### Scoring & Tiers
- Raw score: /50. **Displayed as /100** (raw × 2).
- **S-tier (90-100):** Would genuinely recommend to a stranger. Unique, polished, glasses-native.
- **A-tier (76-89):** Ship proudly. Strong on most dimensions.
- **B-tier (60-75):** Solid but has weaknesses. Improve or accept the gap.
- **C-tier (40-59):** Cut candidate. 2 improvement cycles or remove.
- **F-tier (<40):** Remove. Phone does it better.
- **Minimum score to ship: 60/100**

### Why 10 Dimensions?
Under the old 6-dimension/30-point rubric, 9 of 16 games scored 30/30 (S-tier) because adding rank progression was a universal cheat code. Adding localStorage + ranks reliably pushed Return Visits and D-Pad Fit to 5/5, but didn't improve replayability, depth, or discovery. The 4 new dimensions expose this: Dungeon Crawl (Strategic Depth: 5, Session Variance: 4) now clearly differentiates from Audio Memory (Strategic Depth: 1, Session Variance: 2).

### Lessons from the old system
- **2026-03-12:** Gravity Sling went from 22→25/30 by adding mic-reactive gravity. Pattern: don't bolt audio on as a cosmetic (whoosh sounds). Instead, make it change the core mechanic. Sound modulating gravity means the same level plays differently in a coffee shop vs a library.
- **2026-03-12:** Sonar Sub scored 28/30. Pattern: when the core mechanic IS audio (echolocation), the glasses advantage is maximized. Active ping vs passive sonar creates a resource-management loop.
- **2026-03-12:** Fisher went from 28→29/30 by adding tension audio to the reel fight. Key insight: if your game has a continuous state variable that matters for decisions, it probably needs continuous audio feedback, not just event-driven sounds.
- **2026-03-12:** Signal went from 28→29/30 by adding rank progression. Pattern: cumulative kill milestones give the "just one more game" hook. Each rank bonus is mechanically meaningful, not cosmetic.

## Genre Benchmarks

Every game is scored against its spiritual benchmark — the best game in its genre. A 90/100 MRBD game means it delivers something genuinely compelling within the constraints of 600×600 pixels, D-pad only, and bone conduction audio. This is the difference between "good for a glasses game" and "actually good."

Our games are constrained-format minigames. NetHack has had 40 years of development. Tetris has sold 520 million copies. A Dungeon Crawl score of 88 means it's a tight, well-designed roguelike that uses its constraints creatively — not that it's 88% of NetHack. A score of 95+ would mean the game does something its benchmark *can't* — something only possible on glasses with bone conduction and a mic.

| Game | Genre | Spiritual Benchmark | What the Benchmark Does |
|---|---|---|---|
| dungeon-crawl | Roguelike | NetHack (1987) / Brogue | Procedural levels, permadeath, inventory management, 100+ enemy types, decades of depth |
| sonar-sub | Exploration | Subnautica | Open-world underwater exploration, resource management, genuine terror, hundreds of hours |
| fisher | Relaxation | Stardew Valley (fishing) / Fishing Planet | Hundreds of fish, weather/season systems, gear progression, multiplayer |
| gravity-sling | Physics | Angry Birds / Kerbal Space Program | Intuitive physics, hundreds of levels, deep orbital mechanics |
| trader | Trading | Offworld Trading Company / Wall Street Raider | AI opponents, complex supply/demand, hundreds of commodities, campaign modes |
| stalk | Stealth | Metal Gear Solid / Mark of the Ninja | Enemy AI with patrol routes, multiple approaches, gear/gadgets, story |
| terraform | Simulation | SimCity / Dwarf Fortress | Emergent complexity, hundreds of variables, years of discovery |
| mine | Mining | Motherload / SteamWorld Dig | Upgrade trees, boss fights, biomes, narrative progression |
| signal | Radar | Missile Command / Papers Please | Pattern recognition under pressure, escalating difficulty, narrative weight |
| beacon | Navigation | Return of the Obra Dinn / Scanner Sombre | Audio/visual deduction, environmental storytelling, mystery |
| hex-collapse | Puzzle | Tetris / Hexic | Simple rules + emergent depth, competitive scoring, "one more game" loop |
| photon-dodge | Bullet Hell | Ikaruga / Touhou | Hundreds of patterns, scoring systems, replay value, community |
| rhythm-pulse | Rhythm | Beat Saber / Crypt of the NecroDancer | Song libraries, difficulty curves, leaderboards, physical engagement |
| pulse | Audio | Rez / Tetris Effect | Synesthesia, flow state, progression through audiovisual intensity |
| audio-memory | Memory | Simon / Brain Age | Progressive difficulty, multiple modes, cognitive tracking over time |
| duel | Reflex | Quick Draw (1881) / WarioWare | Microsecond timing, variety, surprise, escalation |
| lord-bluetooth | Action/Defense | Space Invaders / Galaga | Lane-based shooting, wave progression, power-ups, high score chasing |
| lord-bluetooth-org | Management Sim | Game Dev Tycoon / Reigns | Resource management, employee management, event decisions, campaign structure |
| buffalo-brain | Casino/Math | Brain Age / Balatro | Math challenges + resource management + casino mechanics, long-term progression |
| xin-fu-city | City Builder | SimCity / Cities: Skylines | Deep zoning, transportation networks, services, budgets, hundreds of hours of depth |

### How to Use Benchmarks When Scoring
When evaluating a game, ask: "If someone who loves [benchmark] played this for 10 minutes, would they respect it?" Not "is this as good as [benchmark]" — that's an impossible bar for a D-pad minigame. But "does this capture something real about the genre?"

- **A game at 90+** captures the core loop of its benchmark and adds something only glasses can do (mic-as-mechanic, bone conduction spatial audio, ambient-world context).
- **A game at 80-89** is well-made and fun but missing depth that the benchmark has (fewer enemy types, simpler progression, less emergent gameplay).
- **A game at 70-79** works but feels like a tech demo compared to its benchmark (one trick, no discovery, limited replayability).
- **A game below 70** would embarrass you if someone who plays the benchmark tried it.

Same pattern as noise D=5 and metronome D=5. Prior entry stated "All 5 D-pad buttons purposeful" but scored D-pad:4 — inconsistency fixed. Score: 78→80. ## Lessons Log
_Add entries as you learn what works and what doesn't._

- **2026-03-11:** Initial creation. 10 experiences built in first hour — way too fast, quality suffered. Slowed to 2hr create cycles with PM gate.
- **2026-03-11:** Flow fields mic was freaking out — raw audio values cause seizure visuals. Always smooth/lerp audio data (0.12 lerp factor minimum). Winamp vibes > raw reactivity.
- **2026-03-11:** Permissions-Policy blocked mic on Cloudflare Pages. Need `_headers` override for `/experiences/*`. Also getUserMedia throws sync error (not promise rejection) when blocked — must try/catch AND set failure flag before call.
- **2026-03-11:** Terraform's element cycling was impossible to discover — hidden behind moving cursor to grid edge (col 0 or 19). Enter→picker popup is always the right pattern for D-pad element selection.
- **2026-03-11:** Simulation speed matters for engagement. At 1 tick/sec, water+plant combos took forever to grow anything. At 2 ticks/sec with 2× higher growth rates, the ecosystem feels alive within 10-15 seconds of first placement. Don't make the player wait.
- **2026-03-11:** Emoji > ASCII symbols for cell icons on MRBD. 🌳🦊💧 are instantly readable at 26px cells. ♠◆~ are ambiguous.
- **2026-03-12:** Chromatic tuner scored 28/30 — highest experience score. Pattern: "hands are occupied, eyes are free" is the ultimate glasses use case. Musician tools (tuner, metronome) are the archetype. Look for more scenarios where hands are busy (cooking, working out, driving).
- **2026-03-12:** Particle Life was a pure tech demo (no audio, no real trigger). Adding sound-driven rule evolution turned it from "pretty screensaver" to "artificial life that adapts to your environment." The lesson: if an experience doesn't use the mic, ask whether it should. The glasses mic is the differentiator — use it or justify why not.
- **2026-03-12:** Dungeon Crawl is the strongest glasses game — turn-based roguelikes don't need timing, so you can play while doing other things. The minimap toggle (Enter) solved the biggest UX problem (navigating a 40×40 map through a 15×15 viewport). Pattern: turn-based > real-time for glasses. The user is multitasking in the real world; let them play at their pace.
- **2026-03-12:** Synth scored 25/30 after arpeggiator. Pattern: "hold for advanced mode" is a great D-pad UX — tap Enter = simple action, hold Enter = richer mode. Zero extra buttons needed. The arp turns every note into a flowing musical phrase instead of sad single plinks. Scale notes highlighted on keyboard provide visual feedback during arp. Speed control (←→ during arp) lets you dial the energy from meditative (60 BPM) to frantic (400 BPM).
- **2026-03-12:** Spectrum analyzer was purely decorative — pretty bars but no actionable info. Adding BPM detection turned it into a real tool. Pattern: if an experience shows data but doesn't answer a question, it's a screensaver. Every audio tool should tell you something you couldn't figure out by ear alone.
- **2026-03-12:** Audio Memory scored 29/30 — highest game score. The D-pad→tone mapping is the key insight: 4 directions = 4 musical notes is a perfect 1:1 match. The game is nearly playable eyes-free through bone conduction alone. Pattern: when the input device maps perfectly to the game mechanic, the result feels native, not adapted. Look for more 4-element mechanics (card suits, compass dirs, seasons, elements).
- **2026-03-12:** Decibel meter went from TBD→27/30 by adding NIOSH cumulative noise dose. Pattern: tools that answer "should I worry?" beat tools that answer "what's the number?" A gauge showing 95dB is informative; a bar showing "you've used 62% of your daily hearing budget" is actionable. The dose bar uses the same mic data but frames it as a countdown — urgency without alarm fatigue. NIOSH formula (halving every 3dB above 85) is the gold standard; don't invent your own thresholds.
- **2026-03-12:** Photon Dodge went from 19→24/30 by adding mic-reactive bullet patterns. The insight: bullet hells are fundamentally about rhythm, and random timer spawns feel arbitrary. When bullets emerge from ambient sound, every environment produces different gameplay — concert = chaos, quiet room = zen meditation. Bass→heavy rings, mid→precision aimed shots, treble→fast spirals, beat onset→wall bursts. The Winamp visualizer bar at the bottom ties it all together visually. Pattern: if a game has spawn timing, consider whether mic-driven timing would be more engaging.
- **2026-03-12:** Compass went from TBD to 26/30 by adding audio bearing guidance. Pattern: utility experiences that are silent waste the glasses' best output channel (bone conduction speakers). Cardinal clicks feel like a diving bezel — tactile feedback through sound. The "mark bearing" feature adds daily retention: mark your target direction, audio guides you there hands-free. Hiking, exploring a new city, finding your car in a parking lot.
- **2026-03-12:** Hex Collapse (19/30) and Gravity Sling (22/30) are the two weakest games. Both lack audio integration (Audio:2). Hex Collapse specifically has no glasses advantage — it's a perfectly fine phone game that doesn't benefit from being on glasses. Consider either adding mic-reactive mechanics or replacing with something that leverages the glasses context better.
- **2026-03-12:** Intervals timer scored 28/30. Pattern: fitness tools are killer glasses apps because your hands are physically occupied (holding weights, on the floor, gripping bars). The timer metaphor is battle-tested (every gym-goer uses one) — glasses just remove the "pick up phone between sets" friction. Presets are key for D-pad UX: don't make people configure from scratch, give them Tabata/EMOM/etc and let them tweak. Audio transitions (work→rest→next round) are essential — the user is exercising, not staring at the display.
- **2026-03-12:** Morse Trainer scored 28/30. Pattern: "skill training by ear" is a near-perfect glasses use case. The bone conduction speakers deliver audio directly, hands are free (walking, commuting), and the D-pad maps naturally to interaction (←→ for dit/dah in Tap mode, 2×2 grid for Listen mode). The 4-level progression (8→18→26→36 chars) follows standard morse pedagogy (Koch method order: ETIANMSURWDKGO...). Key insight: two modes (Listen=hear→identify, Tap=see→input) train both recognition and production. Audio feedback (ascending chime correct, descending wrong) is critical — the user may not be looking at the display.

- **2026-07-11:** Morse Trainer went from 28→29/30 by adding Speed Challenge mode — 60-second rapid-fire morse identification. The critical insight: "learning tool" experiences score Trigger:4 Hook:4 because the trigger moment is obligation ("I should practice morse") not desire ("I want to play this"). A 60-second timed challenge flips it — the countdown creates urgency, the combo system creates greed, and the grade (S through F) creates ego. The 3-2-1 countdown with escalating beeps sets adrenaline before the first round. Wrong answers subtract 2 seconds (not just break combo) so mistakes HURT — you feel the timer shrink. Combo scoring (each consecutive correct adds +0.5 bonus pts) rewards streaks without being complex. The mode preserves the core mechanic (hear morse → identify letter from 4 options) but wraps it in a competitive shell. Pattern: if a skill-training experience scores Hook:4, add a timed challenge mode. The practice modes stay for learning; the timed mode is for the "60 seconds to kill" trigger moment. Same mechanic, different motivation framing. Trigger:4→5 (specific trigger: "quick brain workout"), Hook:4→5 (countdown + timer + combo = instant excitement).

---
_Update this document every time a game/experience ships, gets user feedback, or teaches you something._
- **2026-03-12:** Starfield was completely silent — a space warp experience with no sound is broken on glasses where bone conduction is a primary output channel. Added procedural engine drone (sawtooth + triangle oscillators through a LPF), wind noise at high speed (white noise through bandpass), and star flyby sounds (sine sweep with stereo panning). The drone pitch shifts with speed (55→130Hz), wind volume ramps above 50% throttle, and flybys trigger when stars pass close to camera center. Pattern: if an experience is fundamentally about motion/speed, it MUST have audio. Silent motion feels uncanny. Score went from TBD to 25/30 — still ambient (low trigger/return) but the audio transforms it from screensaver to meditation.

- **2026-03-12:** Terraform weather system follows gravity-sling pattern: ambient sound modulates a core mechanic (growth rates), not cosmetics. Drought (quiet) kills plants 4× faster and halves all growth — you need more water placement. Downpour (loud) doubles growth and water spread — you can focus on plants and trees. The strategy changes with your environment. Key: use heavy smoothing (0.1 lerp) so weather feels like climate, not a seizure.

- **2026-03-12:** Metronome had a tapTempo() function that was never called — dead code. Wired it to mic transient detection: glasses frame taps create sharp amplitude spikes above the ambient baseline. The pattern: track a slow-moving ambient level (0.005 lerp), detect spikes above threshold (0.35 above ambient), enforce cooldown (150ms). Orange border flash + BPM display color change gives instant feedback. Also added subdivision support (quarter/eighth/triplet on ↑). Score: TBD→27/30. The glasses advantage is real: hands on instrument, tap the frame with your temple or finger to set tempo. No phone needed.

- **2026-03-12:** Pitch Trainer (27/30) — "measurement vs training" is a valid axis for splitting music tools. Tuner answers "is this in tune?" (passive). Pitch Trainer asks "can you match this note?" (active). The hold-to-confirm mechanic (0.5s on pitch) prevents accidental matches and creates a satisfying "lock-in" moment. 4 modes give it longevity: Free for warmup, Scale for learning, Intervals for ear training, Random for challenge. Reference tone playback (Enter) is critical — the user needs to hear the target before matching. Autocorrelation pitch detection with 0.3 lerp smoothing gives stable readings without lag. Pattern: any "measurement" tool (tuner, decibel, spectrum) has a training/challenge variant waiting to be built.

- **2026-03-12:** Dungeon Crawl went from 26→28/30 by adding spatial enemy audio. Pattern: turn-based games with spatial audio are the ultimate glasses experience. You hear a rat scurrying to your left, an orc growling to your right — through bone conduction stereo panning. Because it's turn-based, audio only fires on player movement (no continuous noise). Each enemy type has a distinct sound signature so you can identify threats by ear: rats are high-pitched scurries, skeletons are low rattles, dragons are deep rumbles. The 60% random chance per step prevents audio spam while keeping you aware. Only plays for explored tiles so you can't "cheat" by listening for unexplored areas. This is the Sonar Sub pattern applied to a roguelike — spatial audio through bone conduction as a core gameplay mechanic, not a cosmetic layer.

- **2026-03-12:** Particle Life went from 22→26/30 by adding species selection and extinction. The pattern: simulations without stakes are screensavers. Adding "choose a species to root for" transforms passive observation into active investment. The extinction mechanic (your species dies when it's most isolated) gives the mic-driven evolution real consequences — bass clusters your species to safety, treble scatters and threatens. "Can my species survive in this coffee shop?" is a repeatable trigger that changes with every environment. Population bars provide at-a-glance ecosystem health. Key: extinction must feel fair (based on connectivity ranking, not random death) and recoverable (Enter to retry from species select).

- **2026-03-12:** Photon Dodge went from 24→27/30 by adding actual mic-reactive bullet spawning. The EVALUATE description claimed it was already audio-reactive but the code had ZERO mic integration — patterns spawned on a pure timer. This was the biggest gap between documented and actual state. Lesson: always read the code, not just the inventory table. The mic-to-gameplay mapping (bass=rings, mid=aimed, treble=spirals, beats=walls) creates genuinely different play experiences in different environments. A coffee shop is a bullet storm, a quiet room is meditative dodging. Return score bumped from 3→4 because the environment variability gives you a reason to replay.

- **2026-03-12:** Noise Generator scored 29/30 — tied for highest experience score. Pattern: the killer combo for glasses is bone conduction + ambient awareness. Noise generators on phones require earbuds that block the real world. On glasses, you hear the white noise AND your surroundings — focus without isolation. 8 sounds via Web Audio API procedural generation (no audio files): white (allpass), pink (LP 3kHz), brown (LP 800Hz), ocean (LP with slow LFO), rain (BP 4kHz + HP), stream (BP with fast LFO), wind (BP 500Hz with LFO), fire (LP with sawtooth LFO for crackle). Sleep timer (Space cycles 5/10/15/30/60min + off) auto-stops playback — essential for bedtime use. Visual is minimal: ambient colored particles + breathing ring, matching the current sound's color. The experience is almost entirely audio — perfect for glasses where the display is supplementary.

- **2026-03-12:** Hex Collapse went from 24→26/30 by adding idle valid-group hints. On a 600×600 glasses display, scanning a 9×9 hex grid cell-by-cell looking for matches is tedious. Subtle white dots pulsing on every matchable hex (each group at a different phase to distinguish them) turns the game from "find matches" to "choose the best chain." Match count in the HUD (color-coded: green >3, yellow 1-3, red 0) gives at-a-glance board health. Pattern: puzzle games on glasses should show all available moves — the player's job is strategy, not search. Search is a phone task (you can stare); strategy is a glasses task (you glance and decide).

- **2026-03-12:** Breathe went from TBD→27/30 by adding continuous audio guide. The original had phase chimes at transitions but nothing DURING phases — you had to stare at the expanding/contracting circle to know when to breathe. A gentle sine tone that rises 220→440Hz during inhale, sustains (softer) during hold, and falls 440→220Hz during exhale makes the entire cycle audible through bone conduction. Pattern: "guided" tools that require watching the screen aren't really guided — on glasses, audio IS the guide, visuals are supplementary confirmation. The ←→ toggle lets users turn it off if they want visual-only. Use setTargetAtTime (not setValueAtTime) for smooth frequency ramps that avoid clicks.

- **2026-03-12:** Stalk scored 28/30 — the first stealth game on the platform. The key insight: the mic as stealth mechanic creates a real-world/game boundary blur that's unique to glasses. You're literally sneaking — being quiet IRL to stay hidden in the game. Guards' detection radius scales with mic level (quiet: 2 tiles, loud: 12 tiles), so the game difficulty is directly proportional to your environment. Library = easy, coffee shop = nightmare. The noise glow circle around the player visualizes your "loudness footprint" — players can see how detectable they are. Spatial guard footsteps (stereo panned by position, volume by distance) let you hear patrols through walls before you see them. Pattern: when the real world IS the game mechanic, the glasses advantage is maximum. Not "the game uses the mic for a feature" but "your silence IS your skill."

- **2026-03-12:** Gravity Sling went from 25→27/30 by adding mid-flight thrust. The critical insight: the D-pad was completely idle during the longest phase of gameplay (flight). On glasses where the user should be interacting at a glance, a phase where all you do is watch is wasted time. Thrust costs 0.25 fuel (fractional!) so it's a strategic resource — burn all fuel on thrusts and you have no retries. The visual feedback loop matters: orange flash + exhaust particles + short tone = satisfying micro-interaction. Pattern: if any phase of your game has idle D-pad, consider whether player input during that phase would add strategic depth without complexity.

- **2026-03-12:** Reaction Tester scored 28/30. Pattern: the D-pad IS the game mechanic (4 directions = 4 possible responses). Audio cues encode direction spatially: up=high tone (880Hz), down=low (220Hz), left=left-panned (440Hz), right=right-panned (440Hz). This means in Audio Only mode you can play entirely by ear through bone conduction — genuine glasses advantage over phone. The ms readout is addictive (humans are hardwired to want faster numbers). Session grades (S through D) add a layer beyond raw times. False start detection prevents button mashing. Three modes (Audio+Visual, Audio Only, Visual Only) let users challenge different senses. Records persist in localStorage for daily self-competition. Key insight: "measurement of yourself" tools (reaction time, pitch accuracy, typing speed) have natural daily return — you always want to beat yesterday.

- **2026-03-12:** Earthquakes went from TBD→26/30 by adding seismic audio on browse. Pattern: data utilities benefit from "data sonification" — magnitude as pitch/duration/gain turns a visual scan into a multimodal scan. On glasses you might be walking and scrolling — hearing a deep 1.2s rumble for M7 vs a light 0.6s tick for M3 communicates instantly without reading numbers. The sub-harmonic on M6+ gives a visceral chest-hit that makes big quakes feel BIG through bone conduction.

- **2026-03-12:** Terraform went from 25→27/30 by adding a Planet Health system. Pattern: simulations without a fail state are sandboxes, not games. The health bar decaying creates urgency (place water NOW or the planet dies) without adding complexity — the core loop (place elements → watch ecosystem grow) is identical, but now there's a clock you're racing. Higher levels decay faster, drought accelerates death, rain buys time. The mic-weather system that was previously cosmetic is now strategically important — a loud room literally keeps your planet alive longer. Death + high scores + total-planets-terraformed give three reasons to replay. Key: health should decay slowly enough that the player feels in control but fast enough that idle watching is punished.

- **2026-03-12:** Starfield was pure ambient (Trigger:3, Return:3 — no reason to come back). Adding a distance tracker + waypoint clusters gave it a gentle progress mechanic without ruining the meditative vibe. Key: waypoints are natural-looking star clusters, not rings or checkpoints. Distance is informational ("light-years traveled"), not score pressure. The chime on collection is musical, not gamey. Lesson: even ambient experiences benefit from a tiny bit of persistence (localStorage best distance) — it turns "cool for 5 seconds" into "I'll see if I can beat my best while commuting."

- **2026-03-12:** Signal scored 28/30. Pattern: radar/sonar metaphor maps perfectly to glasses — the circular sweep + spatial audio through bone conduction makes you feel like you're operating equipment, not playing a phone game. Four enemy types with distinct audio signatures (scout=sine, heavy=triangle, dart=square, ghost=pulsing sawtooth) train the player to identify threats by ear. The mic-as-interference mechanic is the inverse of Stalk — instead of "be quiet to survive," it's "noise hides enemies." Both use mic as core mechanic but create opposite strategies. Ammo management prevents spam-firing and forces the player to prioritize. Key: the targeting cursor using ↑↓ for distance and ←→ for angle feels natural on a circular field — better than Cartesian movement would.

- **2026-03-12:** Hex Collapse went from 26→28/30 by mapping hex colors to pentatonic scale notes. Pattern: when the core mechanic is pattern-matching (find same colors), audio that encodes the pattern makes the game partly playable by ear. Pentatonic scale (C-D-E-G-A) means any sequence sounds musical — no dissonance even when scanning randomly. Short tones (80ms) at low volume (0.07) prevent ear fatigue. The ascending arpeggio on match gives visceral feedback proportional to chain size. Key insight: audio shouldn't just confirm actions (beep on move) — it should encode game state (what color am I on?).

- **2026-03-12:** Tinnitus Relief scored 28/30. Pattern: medical/health tools are the highest-utility glasses experiences. The bone conduction advantage is enormous: earbuds that deliver tinnitus therapy also block ambient sound (defeating the purpose of masking), while glasses deliver therapy audio AND let you hear the world. Five modes cover different clinical approaches: notch therapy (bandstop filter removes tinnitus frequency from noise — actual peer-reviewed evidence behind this), white/pink/brown masking (different spectral profiles for personal preference), and pure tone habituation. The hold-to-fast-scroll on frequency tuning is essential — scanning from 500Hz to 16kHz at 100Hz/press would take 155 presses. Session tracking in localStorage adds daily return ("how much therapy have I done this week?"). The notch visualization showing the spectrum gap helps users understand what's happening — it's not just noise, it's shaped noise with their frequency removed.

- **2026-03-12:** Synth went from 25→27/30 by adding a delay effect to the audio chain. Pattern: a dry oscillator is the #1 reason web synths feel like toys. Even a simple delay+feedback+lowpass transforms the sound from 'calculator beep' to 'ambient music.' The delay time (300ms) at 35% feedback gives ~3 audible echoes before dying. Lowpass at 3kHz darkens repeats naturally (like real room reflections). Zero additional UI complexity — just ↓ to toggle. The waveform display gets a ghost trail showing the delay visually. Through bone conduction, the spatial dimension of echoed notes is surprisingly pleasant.

- **2026-03-12:** Gravity Sling went from 27→29/30 by adding named star systems and spatial planet audio. Two changes, tightly coupled: (1) Real star names with distances give levels identity and the 'farthest reached' stat gives a reason to come back (Return:4→5). (2) Planets humming with stereo panning makes them audible landmarks — you can HEAR which side a planet is on through bone conduction (Glasses:4→5). Pattern: progression systems don't need to be complex — just naming things creates attachment. And spatial audio doesn't need to be gameplay-critical to be valuable; ambient spatial awareness through bone conduction is inherently satisfying.

- **2026-03-12:** Trader scored 29/30. Pattern: trading games have the universal "60 seconds to kill" trigger AND a perfectly natural mic integration — ambient sound as market volatility. Loud environments = volatile swings where bold traders profit, quiet = stable markets with steady small gains. The D-pad mapping is crisp: ↑↓ = browse commodities, ← = buy, → = sell. No ambiguity. News events ("FED RATE CUT", "CRYPTO CRASH") inject narrative and momentum shifts that make each round feel like a story. Opening/closing bell audio anchors the 60-second session. Grade system (S through F) + best profit in localStorage = daily return loop. Key insight: "your environment IS the game variable" is the platform's strongest pattern — Stalk (quiet=safe), Signal (noise=interference), Terraform (sound=weather), and now Trader (noise=volatility) all prove it.

- **2026-03-12:** Spectrum analyzer went from 26→28/30 by adding peak frequency identification + note name + reference tone playback. Pattern: audio tools that just visualize are passive — adding an "identify" action (show me the frequency, name the note, play it back) turns passive watching into active investigation. The reference tone playback (Enter → sine at detected peak) lets you A/B compare what you're hearing against the measured frequency — useful for ear training, speaker testing, room acoustics. Parabolic interpolation on FFT bins gives sub-bin accuracy without autocorrelation overhead. Heavy smoothing (0.7 lerp) is critical for frequency displays — raw FFT peak bin jumps around violently.

- **2026-03-12:** Terraform went from 27→29/30 by adding a 4-layer ambient ecosystem soundscape. Pattern: simulation games have a "watch and wait" phase where the player has placed elements and is watching the system evolve. This phase was completely silent — which is broken on glasses where bone conduction is a primary output channel. The fix: continuous audio layers whose volume scales proportionally with element counts. Wind (filtered white noise) is always subtle; water (bandpass noise) fades in with water cells; forest (triangle drone chord at 110Hz+165Hz) grows with trees; life (FM chirps at 2-8Hz) scales with animals. Critical details: (1) All layers max at 0.02-0.035 volume — they layer UNDER the existing UI event sounds, not on top. (2) setTargetAtTime for smooth fades, never clicks. (3) Weather modulates the wind filter frequency so rain/drought sounds different. (4) Mute on death/win/menu so audio doesn't persist into non-game states. The lesson generalizes: any simulation with a "watch phase" should have continuous audio encoding the system state. The player's ears are free while they look elsewhere.
- **2026-03-12:** Particle Life went from 26→28/30 by adding shepherd mechanic. The insight: simulation experiences where you pick a side but then watch passively have a huge engagement gap — the sim runs for minutes but your only interaction was a one-time species pick. Adding a D-pad cursor that attracts your species gives continuous agency without disrupting the emergent behavior. The cursor influence fades with distance (120px radius) and uses the same force system as the sim, so it feels organic not artificial. Key lesson: if >50% of an experience is passive watching, that's where the improvement should go. Don't add more menus or modes — add agency to the idle phase.

- **2026-03-12:** Neck Stretch scored 28/30. Pattern: the glasses ARE a sensor, not just a display. deviceorientation API gives real-time head pitch/yaw/roll — no other wearable tracks head position this naturally. The calibration step (3-second "look straight ahead") establishes a baseline so all movements are relative. Guide tone (pitch drops as you approach target) makes it partly eyes-free — you can close your eyes and navigate by sound. The hold phase (gentle pulsing tone) gives a different audio cue from the approach phase. ROM tracking gives measurable progress across sessions. Key: wellness/health tools with daily triggers AND measurable progress have the strongest return-visit potential. Session count + streak tracking in localStorage reinforces the habit loop.

- **2026-03-12:** Photon Dodge went from 27→28/30 by adding a phase system (CALM→RISING→STORM→CHAOS→HELLFIRE). The lesson: survival games with a flat difficulty ramp feel endless and forgettable. Named phases turn each run into a story with acts. "I made it to HELLFIRE!" is something you tell people; "I scored 4,827" is not. The phases also give the designer clear escalation knobs (spawn rate ×0.7→×2.0, bullet speed ×0.85→×1.5) instead of a single linear ramp. Chime + flash + big text on transition creates a moment of "oh no, it just got real." Return score bumped from 4→5 because players now have a named milestone to chase.

- **2026-03-12:** Fisher scored 28/30 — fills the "chill" gap in the roster. Every other game is either fast-paced or complex. The fishing metaphor works because the waiting IS the gameplay: tension builds with nibbles (directional bubble audio), then the bite demands a quick reaction. The reel fight adds a skill layer without complexity — just keep the tension meter centered. Mic integration: quiet room = fish frenzy (3-5 second waits), loud room = fish spooked (8-12 second waits, but catches are rarer/more valuable). The 25-species fish log with rarity stars creates Pokemon-style collection drive. Pattern: "relaxation games" still need a moment of tension — cast→wait→BITE!→reel is a perfect tension arc that repeats every 10-20 seconds. The environmental depth spots (Pond→Deep Sea) gate species rarity naturally.
- **2026-03-12:** Flow Fields was TBD→27/30. Pattern: generative ambient audio synthesis is the ideal complement to generative art on glasses. Four oscillators (open fifth drone: C2/G2/C3/G3) with frequencies modulated by the average flow direction in each screen quadrant creates a direct visual→audio mapping — the same Perlin noise that drives the particles also drives the pitch. Mic bass opens the filter (warm), treble brightens it. The result: room→mic→visual→audio→you is a full feedback loop. The meditation potential is real — put on the glasses, press any key, close your eyes, and the ambient sound of your environment shapes a unique drone soundscape. Key implementation detail: use `setTargetAtTime` (not direct assignment) for all audio parameter changes — smooth ramps prevent clicks/pops. Keep oscillator count low (4) and volumes very low (0.06 per osc, 0.04-0.07 master gain) — glasses speakers are inches from your ears.

- **2026-03-12:** Dungeon Crawl went from 28→29/30 by adding floating combat numbers and tile flashes. The game had audio feedback but zero visual feedback for combat — you'd walk into an enemy and only the HUD text changed. Floating numbers (-3 orange rising from the enemy, -2 red rising from you) plus tile flash effects give instant at-a-glance feedback. On glasses where the user may be multitasking, a red flash = "I'm taking damage" is readable in peripheral vision. Pattern: even in turn-based games, combat needs visual punctuation. Audio + visual feedback together > either alone. Shadow text (dark outline behind light text) is essential for readability over varied tile backgrounds.

- **2026-03-12:** Earthquakes went from 26→28/30 by adding proximity-first sort + "Did you feel it?" estimation. Pattern: data visualization tools that answer "does this affect ME?" are fundamentally more engaging than generic global views. The trigger moment changes from "I want to browse earthquake data" (weak) to "was that a quake?" (strong). When geolocation is available, sorting by distance means the first quake you see is always the most relevant one. The MMI estimation (magnitude × distance × depth attenuation) gives a human-readable answer instead of raw numbers. Lesson: if an experience shows global data and the user has location, make it personal by default.

- **2026-03-12:** Posture Coach scored 28/30. Pattern: "passive background monitoring" is a distinct category from "active guided sessions" (like Neck Stretch). Neck Stretch is 2-4 minute exercises you do intentionally. Posture Coach runs all day, nudging when you slouch. The deviceorientation API gives real head tilt data — calibrate once, then the glasses track deviation continuously. Key design decisions: (1) Gentle double-tap tone for nudges (not alarming — you'll wear this all day). (2) 10-second nudge cooldown prevents nagging. (3) 5 sensitivity levels let users dial from loose (25° threshold) to strict (7°). (4) History chart shows posture trends over the last 2 minutes — patterns emerge (always slouch after 10 min). (5) Good-posture chime when you straighten up provides positive reinforcement. Session grades (A+→F) + streak tracking + all-time stats in localStorage give daily return. The glasses advantage is maximum: the sensor IS the device, it's already on your head, and the nudge is bone conduction private audio — nobody else hears it.
- **2026-03-12:** Sonar Sub went from 28→29/30 by adding depth-unlock progression. The game had no meta-progression — once you'd gone deep, there was no reason to come back. 8 permanent upgrades unlocked at milestone depths (100m–2000m) give each run a "just one more dive" hook. Key insight: roguelike progression works because upgrades are small enough to not trivialize the game (+2 ping range, +15% oxygen) but meaningful enough to notice. The unlock chime + 3-second overlay is minimal interruption. Pattern: if a game has a high score but no persistent growth, add milestone unlocks. The progression curve (100→200→350→500→750→1000→1500→2000) accelerates early to hook, then spaces out to retain. Return score went from 4→5.
- **2026-03-12:** Compass went from 26→28/30 by replacing continuous sine bearing tone with stereo-panned Geiger clicks. Pattern: continuous tones are fatiguing and tell you one thing ("you are off"). Rhythmic clicks encode TWO things: distance (click rate) and direction (stereo pan). The bone conduction speakers on glasses have real spatial separation — stereo panning actually works for left/right guidance. This transforms bearing guidance from "look at the screen" to "close your eyes and turn until the clicks center and speed up." The Geiger counter metaphor is universally understood — faster clicks = closer — so zero learning curve.

- **2026-03-12:** Mine scored 29/30. Pattern: "listen before you act" creates a discovery mechanic from audio alone. Proximity sounds (gems chime, gas hisses, hard rock clunks) at 2-tile range with stereo panning mean the player can orient toward treasure or away from danger by ear through bone conduction — before they even see it. The mic-as-difficulty mechanic (louder room = harder rock) follows the platform pattern of "environment IS the game variable." Pickaxe durability as the core tension (every swing costs HP, gas deals bonus damage) gives a natural game-over condition without health bars or enemies. Key insight: digging games have the same satisfying loop as roguelikes (go deeper, find stuff, die, try again) but simpler inputs — just 4 directions + mine. Enter = dig down for one-hand play. Depth records + gem collection + runs counter give three retention hooks.

- **2026-03-12:** Focus Timer scored 28/30. Pattern: "passive productivity HUD" is a strong glasses category. The key insight is that a focus timer on your phone requires breaking focus to check it — defeating the purpose. On glasses, the progress arc sits in peripheral vision. You never pick anything up, never look away from your work. Bone conduction ticks at escalating frequency (every 60s → 30s → 10s as time runs low) create subconscious urgency without alarm. The last-minute red glow is visible in peripheral vision. Auto-cycling through focus→break→focus removes all decision fatigue. Session dots (4 before long break) give visual progress toward a reward. Daily session count + best streak in localStorage provide return hooks. Simple feature: ←→ adjusts focus time, ↑↓ switches to break time adjustment — two controls, full customization. The ascending C-E-G chime on completion feels like an achievement; the descending G-E-C on break end gently pulls you back.

- **2026-03-12:** Decibel meter went from 27→29/30 by adding continuous audio feedback. Pattern: a *measurement* tool should be usable without *looking* at it. The decibel meter was a visual gauge — ironic for a sound measurement app. A sine tone that tracks dB level through bone conduction (150Hz quiet → 1200Hz loud, quadratic curve so danger frequencies accelerate) turns it into an ears-free monitor. LFO pulse above 85dB provides warning without words. Dose alerts (double/triple beeps at 50%/90% exposure) are event-driven, not continuous — important distinction so they actually grab attention. Key: the continuous tone is very quiet (0.02-0.08 gain) so it doesn't contribute to the noise it's measuring.

- **2026-03-12:** Beacon scored 28/30. Pattern: "guardian" games where you protect others instead of yourself create a different emotional register than "survive" games. The lighthouse metaphor works because the beam is the core mechanic AND the visibility mechanic — you can only see what you illuminate. The tradeoff between beam width and range (↑=wider but shorter, ↓=narrower but longer) gives a single control axis with meaningful decisions: wide beam to scan, narrow to reach distant ships. Mic-as-fog follows the platform pattern (environment changes the game) but the mapping is more intuitive than most — loud room = foggy, quiet room = clear — because fog and noise both reduce information. Spatial foghorns (stereo-panned by ship position, volume by distance) let you locate ships by ear before sweeping the beam, creating a hear-then-see loop that's deeply satisfying on bone conduction. Night progression (more rocks + more ships + faster spawns) provides natural difficulty without artificial complexity. The "ships steer away from rocks when illuminated" mechanic means the beam isn't just revealing — it's actively saving ships, which feels good.

- **2026-03-12:** Flow Fields went from 27→29/30 by adding ambient mood classification. Pattern: a "pretty screensaver" has no trigger moment — nobody thinks "I should open flow fields right now." But "what does this café register as?" IS a trigger moment. The mood label (Still/Calm/Social/Energetic/Musical) turns passive watching into active environmental curiosity. Implementation: classify using existing mic data (level for volume, bass/treble ratio for content type, variance over 60 frames for rhythmic activity). Heavy smoothing on all classification inputs (0.05 lerp) prevents flickering between states — a café that reads as "Social" should stay "Social" for seconds, not oscillate every frame. The mood colors (gray→teal→gold→red→purple) and emoji (🌙🍃☕⚡🎵) give instant visual differentiation. Key insight: if an experience has "no reason to open it," the fix isn't adding more features — it's giving the user a *question* they want answered. The question IS the trigger.

- **2026-03-13:** Stalk went from 28→29/30 by adding agent rank progression (8 ranks: Rookie→Unseen). Pattern: same as Signal and Sonar Sub — cumulative milestone progression drives "one more game" retention. The stealth-specific twist: ranks are earned exclusively via ghost runs (zero-detection clears), which reinforces the core stealth identity. Each rank bonus is mechanically meaningful: peek duration, noise dampening, FOV, extra detections. The getRankBonus() system with stepped thresholds (not linear scaling) means each rank unlock is a noticeable jump, not a gradual fade. Title screen rank display with progress bar creates anticipation before the game even starts. Key: progression should reward the game's CORE skill (stealth/ghost runs), not a secondary metric (total missions, steps). Return:4→5.

- **2026-03-13:** Breathe went from 27→29/30 by adding biofeedback sync scoring. The original had breath detection code (mic trend analysis) that detected inhale/exhale but DID NOTHING with the result — classic dead feature. The fix: compare mic volume trend (rising/falling/stable) against the current guide phase (inhale/exhale/hold) and compute a real-time sync score. Visual sync ring around the breathing circle turns green when you're in sync. A harmony tone (triangle wave, perfect fifth above the guide sine) fades in proportionally to sync quality — breathing in sync literally sounds more musical through bone conduction. Session summary with grade (S→F), average sync %, best streak, and persistent best per pattern. Pattern: if an experience has sensor data flowing but no feedback loop, the fix is CLOSING THE LOOP. Measurement without feedback is a tech demo. Measurement with feedback is biofeedback — and biofeedback is inherently addictive because you're competing against yourself. Every Hook:4 experience should ask: "am I measuring something the user would want to improve?"

- **2026-03-13:** Tally scored 28/30. Pattern: "pure utility" experiences that solve a universal micro-problem are the strongest glasses apps. Everyone counts things — reps at the gym, people at a door, birds on a hike — and your hands are ALWAYS occupied when you need to count (holding a barbell, binoculars, clipboard). The multi-counter with targets gives it depth: set a goal for 50 reps, watch the progress ring fill, hear the milestone chimes at 10/25/50. The ascending click pitch (300Hz→700Hz as count grows) provides satisfying audio progression — you can hear how far along you are without looking. Key: presets (Reps/People/Birds/Drinks/Laps) answer "what is this for?" instantly. Daily persistence means the counter resets each day but tracks best-day and all-time. Zero learning curve — press Enter, number goes up.

- **2026-03-13:** Beacon went from 28→29/30 by adding keeper rank progression (8 ranks: Wick Lighter→Harbor Master). Pattern confirmed for the fourth time: cumulative milestone progression with mechanical bonuses (not just cosmetic) is the universal Return:4→5 fix. The lighthouse keeper metaphor makes ranks feel earned and thematic rather than gamified — you're literally earning promotions as a keeper. The bonus stacking (beam range, rotation speed, starting width, wreck tolerance) gives higher-ranked players a noticeably stronger lighthouse, which creates a feel-good power curve without making early ranks frustrating. Key: bonuses should enhance the core mechanic (beam sweeping), not add new mechanics. The rank system took Signal, Sonar Sub, Stalk, and now Beacon from 28→29 — it's the one intervention that reliably works.

- **2026-03-13:** Starfield went from 27→29/30 by adding Voyager Mode — a 5-destination navigation challenge driven entirely by audio beacons through bone conduction. Pattern: passive ambient experiences with no goal structure have a Return ceiling at 4/5 — you can't want to "come back tomorrow" to a screensaver. The fix isn't adding enemies or pressure (that breaks the ambient identity), it's adding a **parallel goal mode** that coexists with Free Roam. Voyager Mode chains 5 star system destinations. Each emits a stereo-panned beacon tone: panning tells you direction, frequency tells you distance (330Hz far → 880Hz on arrival). You steer with ←→ and speed with ↑↓ — same controls, completely different purpose. Arrival triggers ascending chime, completion plays a four-chord fanfare, best times persist. The key insight: a continuous sine beacon through bone conduction creates a **navigation feeling** that no visual indicator can match — you genuinely feel like you're "homing in" on something. Direction chevrons on screen edges (1-3 based on bearing offset) plus centered reticle when aligned provide visual confirmation, but the audio leads. Enter cycles through three states: Free Roam → Mic Mode → Voyager → back to Free Roam. The mode toggle means both audiences are served — meditative drifters and competitive navigators — without either mode polluting the other. Lesson: when an experience scores uniformly 4/5 across all dimensions, the fix is adding a **goal layer** that transforms one dimension from "nice" to "compelling" without touching the base experience.

- **2026-03-14:** Rhythm Pulse went from 28→29/30 by wiring up persistent rank progression. The rank scaffolding (8 ranks, bonuses, loadStats/saveStats) was already in the code but completely disconnected — bonuses never applied to scoring or speed, title screen showed no rank, game over showed no progress. Rank system was a dead feature. The fix: apply getRankBonuses() in hitLane (wider perfect window, higher combo multiplier, point multiplier), update loop (speed multiplier), and startGame (round duration). Added title screen rank display with progress bar to next rank, game over rank progress % with rank-up announcement, HUD rank label, and ascending C-E-G-C chime on rank up. Pattern confirmed for 5th time: rank progression is the universal Return:4→5 fix — and for rhythm games specifically, musical bonuses (perfect window, combo multiplier) feel better than stat buffs because they reward precision, which is what the player is already practicing.

- **2026-03-14:** Metronome went from 27→28/30 (Audio:4→5) by replacing thin sine-wave clicks with woodblock-style synthesized clicks and adding swing subdivision. The old clicks were 80ms sine oscillators — fine for testing, terrible for 20-minute practice sessions through bone conduction. New clicks use a noise burst transient (15ms filtered white noise for attack snap) + resonant triangle-wave body (60ms exponential decay with pitch drop). Downbeat clicks are sharper and louder (freq 1600Hz, Q=3), subdivision clicks are softer and rounder (1100Hz, Q=1.5). Also added swing as 4th subdivision mode (quarter→eighth→triplet→swing via ↑) — swing offbeat fires at 2/3 of the beat interval, standard jazz triplet-swing feel. Lesson: for sustained-use audio tools, click timbre is not cosmetic — it's the product. A noise transient gives the "stick hitting wood" attack that sine waves can't provide, and the resonant body gives warmth without muddiness. The frequency separation between downbeat and subdivision clicks matters for distinguishability through bone conduction where stereo imaging is limited.

- **2026-04-08:** Duel 88 — CEO PIP deep analysis found 3 of 6 modifiers were cosmetic (FOG/MIRROR/DUST had zero gameplay impact — only visual effects that didn't affect the audio-tell core mechanic). Fix: (1) FOG now muffles draw sound to 8% volume — tell exists but is barely perceptible, especially in noisy environments with mic-based difficulty. (2) MIRROR now inverts the audio-tell dynamic: bluffs play the draw sound, real draws are silent. Visual color becomes the tell (orange=bluff, green=real). Forces complete strategy reversal — trained players must override "sound = real" instinct. (3) DUST STORM now plays phantom noise pops (short square-wave bursts in similar frequency range) every 0.8-2.3s during wait phase, creating auditory false positives. Each modifier now targets a distinct axis: SILENCE removes audio, FOG degrades it, MIRROR inverts it, DUST adds noise, QUICK DRAW reduces time, DOUBLE BLUFF increases bluff rate. Pattern: modifiers that only change visuals in an audio-tell game are fake variance. Real variance means different optimal information-processing strategies. Session Variance: 3→4. Raw score now 45/50 = 90 but scored at 88 per AI scoring cap; flagged as S-tier candidate for Ray's confirmation.
- **2026-04-10:** Duel score correction: Strategic Depth 4→3 (raw 45→44/50, displayed 88 unchanged). The prior cycle scored Strategic Depth at 4 because modifiers create different processing strategies. But "different processing strategies" is Session Variance, not Strategic Depth. The rubric: Depth 3 = "some tactical choices", Depth 4 = approaching resource management and multi-axis decisions. Duel's in-game decision is binary (press/don't press) with bluff detection as the tactical layer. Compare tower-defense (Depth 4): gold management, tower type × placement × upgrade decisions, mutation adaptation — multiple interacting choices per turn. Duel's modifier system is correctly captured by Session Variance (4) and Surprise (4), not Strategic Depth. Removes misleading "S-tier candidate" flag. QUALITY.md already stated "Does not warrant S-tier: strategic depth is fundamentally binary" — this correction aligns EVALUATE.md with that honest assessment. Pattern: when a score disagrees with its own editorial judgment, the score is wrong.
- **2026-03-14:** Duel was unlisted in EVALUATE.md (no score). Added to inventory at 28/30 after adding gunslinger rank progression. 8 ranks (Greenhorn→Legend) based on cumulative wins, each granting a gameplay bonus: wider draw window (+50ms per early rank) and faster countdown at Ranger+. Pattern confirmed again: rank progression is the universal Return fix. The western theme makes ranks feel thematic — "I'm a Sheriff, pushing for Ranger" is stickier than "I have 50 wins." Key design: window bonus doesn't make the game easier in a way that matters — LEGENDARY threshold is still <180ms regardless of window. The bonus just prevents frustrating timeout deaths at higher ranks, letting you focus on chasing faster times. The ascending C-E-G-C chime on rank up fires when you advance from the result screen, creating a moment of celebration between duels. Two games (Duel, Pulse) were in games/ but not in EVALUATE — always check for unlisted games during improvement passes.

- **2026-03-14:** Steps went from 27→29/30 by adding cadence audio feedback. The step counter was the platform's flagship "glasses advantage" experience (phone stays in pocket, just glance) — but it was SILENT while walking. On glasses where your eyes are on the world, not a screen, a step counter you can't hear defeats the purpose. The fix: every step plays a subtle triangle-wave tick (0.02 gain, 50ms) whose frequency encodes pace zone: STROLL (220Hz, gentle), WALK (330Hz, natural), BRISK (440Hz, energizing), RUN (550Hz, urgent). Zone transitions play a two-tone glide (old freq → new freq) with floating notification. Every 500 steps, a progress chime plays ascending major 3rd if on pace for daily goal (time-of-day projection) or descending if behind. ↑ toggles cadence audio (persisted in localStorage), ↓ plays on-demand progress check — two new D-pad functions that were idle before. Pace zone bar below stats visualizes current zone at a glance. Pattern: fitness tools on glasses MUST have audio encoding the key metric (pace for steps, phase for intervals, tension for exercises). A visual-only fitness tool on glasses is worse than a phone app because the phone is in your hand during workouts anyway — the glasses advantage is ears-free, eyes-free feedback through bone conduction. The tick pitch must be very quiet (0.02 gain) and very short (50ms) so sustained walking doesn't cause ear fatigue — it should feel like a heartbeat rhythm, not an alarm. Smooth pace averaging (0.7 lerp) prevents zone flicker at boundaries.

- **2026-03-14:** Synth went from 27→29/30 by adding a mic-reactive lowpass filter. Pattern: a synth on glasses is just a worse phone synth unless the environment shapes the sound. Ambient level modulating filter cutoff (200Hz quiet → 8kHz loud) and bass content driving resonance (Q up to 10 for acid squelch) means the same arpeggio sounds completely different in a quiet room vs a café vs a concert. The exponential cutoff mapping (200 × 40^level) is essential — linear mapping clusters most of the audible range in a tiny level band. Heavy smoothing on ALL mic inputs (0.06 lerp level, 0.04 bass, 0.08 cutoff, 0.06 Q) prevents the filter from jittering, which would click/pop through bone conduction. The filter visualizer strip (colored gradient bar + cutoff line + resonance glow) gives visual feedback without cluttering the keyboard view. Key insight: routing ALL audio (dry notes, delay wet, arp) through a single mic-reactive filter means the feature enhances every existing mode for free — no per-mode wiring needed. The old mic visualizer mode was removed because it was redundant with the spectrum experience. Glasses:4→5, Return:4→5.

- **2026-03-14:** Pitch Trainer went from 27→28/30 by adding vocal rank progression (8 ranks: Listener→Perfect Pitch). Pattern confirmed for 7th time: cumulative milestone progression is the universal Return fix. The pitch-training twist: rank bonuses directly improve the matching mechanic — wider in-tune window (+20%→+80%) and faster hold-to-confirm (30→12 frames). This is crucial because early ranks of pitch training are frustrating (narrow window + long hold = hard to feel progress). The bonuses don't remove the skill ceiling (you still need to hit the right pitch) but they reduce the frustration floor. Stats now persist in localStorage (totalHits, bestStreak, sessions) — previously ALL stats reset on page reload, which was the single biggest retention killer. The progress bar showing "47/120 → Harmonist" on the title area creates the "one more note" anticipation loop. Hook:4→5.

- **2026-03-14:** Hex Collapse went from 28→29/30 by adding puzzle rank progression (8 ranks: Novice→Chromatic) based on cumulative total score. Pattern confirmed for 8th time: rank progression is the universal Return:4→5 fix. The puzzle-specific twist: three bonus types — score multiplier (1.2×→1.5×), combo start (combo resets to 1 instead of 0, so first match is always x2), and bomb cooldown reduction (75%→50%). The combo start bonus is the most interesting because it amplifies the existing chain mechanic — skilled players who chain multiple matches benefit more from a higher starting combo than from raw score multipliers. Bomb cooldown reduction rewards players who use the mic tap feature, reinforcing the glasses-specific mechanic. Key: bonuses that synergize with existing mechanics (combos, sound bombs) feel more integrated than additive stats. Return:4→5.

- **2026-03-14:** Intervals went from 28→29/30 by adding continuous heartbeat pulse during work/rest phases. The critical insight: a HIIT timer that's silent during the 20-second work phase is USELESS on glasses — you're face-down doing burpees, you can't see the progress ring. The heartbeat encodes two things through bone conduction: (1) Phase identity — work pulses are higher pitch (400-800Hz) and accelerating (72→200 BPM), rest pulses are low/steady (300Hz, 50 BPM). You know which phase you're in by ear alone. (2) Progress — the accelerating BPM during work creates exponential urgency in the last 30%, making "almost done" FEEL different from "just started" without any visual. The halfway double-tap at 50% is the key landmark — on a 20-second Tabata set, knowing you're at the halfway point keeps you pushing. Pattern: any timer-based experience should have continuous audio encoding the countdown, not just transition beeps. The existing transition beeps (work/rest/done) are EVENT audio; the heartbeat is STATE audio — they serve different information needs. Both are necessary. Keep pulse volumes low (0.06-0.14 gain) so they layer under existing beeps. Hook:4→5, Audio:4→5.

- **2026-06-10:** Photon Dodge went from 28→29/30 by adding pilot rank progression (8 ranks: Cadet→Legend) with persistent localStorage stats. This was the last game without ANY persistence — hiScore and bestPhase vanished on page reload, which is the #1 retention killer. The arcade-specific twist: rank bonuses enhance the two core survival mechanics — shield duration (+15%→+40%), shield cooldown reduction (-20%→-40%), and graze zone expansion (+15%→+35%). Graze zone expansion is the most interesting because it increases risk/reward: a bigger graze zone means more grazing opportunities (more score) but also means you're spending more time near bullets. Shield bonuses reward aggressive play at higher ranks without reducing difficulty (bullet count/speed unchanged). Pattern confirmed for 9th time: rank progression is the universal Return:4→5 fix. Also added Pulse (heartbeat rhythm game) to EVALUATE.md — it was in games/ but unlisted, scored 28/30.

- **2026-06-10:** Tuner went from 28→29/30 by adding reference tone playback, in-tune chime, and instrument presets. The tuner had ZERO audio output — a music tool on bone conduction glasses that was completely silent. Three additions: (1) Enter toggles a sine reference tone at the nearest detected note's target frequency. The tone tracks note changes smoothly (setTargetAtTime with 0.08 time constant) so if you're tuning from E2 to A2, the reference slides with you. Volume at 0.06 — audible through bone conduction but doesn't interfere with mic pickup. (2) In-tune chime: after 60 consecutive frames (~1 second) within ±3 cents, a gentle two-note triangle wave chime (note + octave above) confirms the string is locked without looking. The chime only fires once per tune attempt — resets when you go out of tune. (3) ↑↓ cycles through 6 instrument presets (Guitar Standard, Drop D, Bass, Ukulele, Violin, Chromatic) with matching string frequencies shown at the bottom. Detected note highlights matching strings with green glow. Previously ↑↓ and Enter were completely unused — 3/5 D-pad buttons were dead. Pattern: music tools on glasses MUST have audio output, not just audio input. The ref tone creates an A/B comparison loop (hear the target, adjust your instrument, hear it converge) that's impossible on a silent visual-only tuner when your eyes are on your instrument. Hook:4→5 (instrument presets give a reason to explore), D-pad:4→5 (all buttons active), Audio:5 confirmed.

- **2026-07-11:** Tally went from 28→29/30 by adding mic-tap counting. The core insight: Tally's trigger moment is "hands are busy, need to count" (holding a barbell, binoculars, clipboard) — but it still required pressing Enter with your hand. That's a contradiction. Mic transient detection (RMS spike above slow-moving ambient baseline, 350ms cooldown) lets you count by clapping, tapping the glasses frame, or making any sharp sound. The implementation: ambient level tracks via ultra-slow lerp (0.004) so it adapts to any environment — loud gym, quiet trail. Threshold is 0.30 above ambient, which catches deliberate taps while ignoring conversation and music. 350ms cooldown prevents double-counts from reverb. Tap counts get distinct audio feedback: noise-burst transient (15ms bandpass-filtered white noise for a "woodblock tap" attack) + triangle-wave pitched confirmation — audibly different from D-pad counts so you always know HOW a count was registered. Visual feedback: radial gradient flash + green mic indicator that goes yellow on trigger. Toggle in menu (persisted in localStorage), disabled during menu/edit modes. Pattern: if a utility's trigger moment involves occupied hands, the utility MUST offer hands-free input. D-pad is a fallback, not the primary interaction. Mic transient detection is the universal hands-free input for glasses — same pattern as metronome's tap tempo. Audio:4→5.

- **2026-07-11:** Compass went from 28→29/30 by adding a saved waypoint library. The critical insight: "mark bearing → navigate → clear" is a one-shot interaction with zero persistence — there's no reason to come back tomorrow. A saved waypoint library (up to 8, persisted in localStorage) transforms the compass from a single-use tool into a personal navigation system. ↑↓ cycles through saved waypoints (replacing the marginal level-bubble toggle that occupied ↑↓ before), each with pitch-encoded audio feedback (triangle wave, pitch rising with list position). All saved waypoints appear as subtle blue dots on the compass ring, giving at-a-glance context of your directional landscape. Near-duplicate detection (within 3°) prevents clutter from re-marking the same bearing. ←→ adjusts the active waypoint's bearing and persists the change. The "mark + save" gesture (Enter) is identical to before — zero learning curve increase — but now every mark builds your library. Pattern: utility experiences that are "use and discard" (mark → go → clear) have a Return ceiling at 4/5. Adding persistent state that accumulates across sessions ("my saved waypoints") transforms a tool into a personal artifact. The waypoint library is the compass equivalent of the fish log, depth records, or rank progression — it gives the tool a memory. D-pad:4→5 (all 5 buttons now purposeful), Return:4→5 (waypoint library creates daily value).

- **2026-07-11:** Pulse went from 28→29/30 by adding a continuous audio guide metronome at the target BPM rate. The critical insight: a rhythm game where you can only SEE the target BPM as a number requires mental math ("65 BPM = one press per ~0.9 seconds") — that's not rhythm, that's arithmetic. A continuous woodblock-style tick at the target rate through bone conduction turns it into "hear the beat, sync your presses" — the fundamental rhythm game mechanic. Implementation: each condition has a distinct pitch (600Hz–1200Hz mapped to condition color) so you can identify the condition AND the rhythm by ear alone. Noise transient attack (8ms filtered white noise) + resonant triangle body (50ms with pitch drop) gives a satisfying click that doesn't fatigue. Volumes kept very low (0.03 gain) so the guide layers under the louder lub-dub heartbeat audio. Guide re-syncs (guideNextTick=0) on condition change so the new rhythm starts clean. ↑ toggles guide on/off (persisted in localStorage) for players who want pure challenge. Pattern: any rhythm game on glasses MUST have an audible target rhythm — on a HUD where you're not staring at the screen, audio IS the guide. Without it, the game was a timing puzzle with a number; with it, it's a rhythm game you can feel. Glasses:4→5 (nearly eyes-free with guide enabled).

- **2026-07-11:** Neck Stretch went from 24→26/30 by adding rank progression (8 ranks: Beginner→Zen Master). This was improvement attempt 2 of 2 for a B-tier experience. The key insight: neck stretching has a natural "progression" feel — you literally get more flexible over time — but the old version had zero persistent measurement of improvement. The rank system maps cumulative sessions to two bonuses that directly reduce the biggest gameplay frustration: (1) wider hold zone threshold (+1°→+4°) means experienced users don't have to hold their head as precisely, matching real-world flexibility gains; (2) softer drift penalty (2.0×→0.3× decay multiplier) means momentary drifts during holds don't erase progress as harshly. These bonuses feel earned, not gamified — a Zen Master who's done 100 sessions genuinely has better neck control and deserves a wider target zone. Best ROM per axis now persists in localStorage with "NEW BEST" indicators on the complete screen, giving each session a measurable outcome beyond "done." Title screen shows rank + progress bar, complete screen shows rank-up announcement with blue glow + ascending chime. Pattern confirmed: rank progression works for wellness/fitness experiences too, not just games. The thresholds (3/8/15/25/40/60/100 sessions) are spaced to reward daily practice — a user doing one session per day unlocks Dedicated in ~2 weeks and Limber in ~1 month. Return:4→5 (progression + ROM records give reason to come back).

- **2026-07-11:** Particle Life went from 22→25/30 by adding a 6-oscillator ecosystem audio drone layer. The critical insight: an experience with ZERO audio output on glasses is like a movie with no soundtrack — technically functional but emotionally dead. Six persistent oscillators (pentatonic C3-C4, alternating sine/triangle waveforms) each track one species' connectivity. Thriving species hum louder, isolated ones go quiet. The chosen species is amplified 1.4× while others dim to 0.3×, creating audio focus on "your" species. Master gain scales with total ecosystem health (0.04-0.14) — a dying ecosystem literally sounds quieter. Event sounds add punctuation: pentatonic selection tones when browsing species, ascending bond chime (root-3rd-5th-octave) on species pick, descending sawtooth sweep with LP filter on extinction, and intensity-scaled mutation ticks on rule changes. All at conservative bone conduction volumes. Pattern: continuous audio encoding system state (ecosystem connectivity → drone volume) is more valuable than event-only audio (chimes on actions) because the continuous layer works while you're NOT looking at the screen. The drone tells you "your species is struggling" through bone conduction before you glance down. Both are needed but the continuous layer is what makes it glasses-native. Audio:2→4 (continuous ecosystem + events, still not playable by ear alone). Still B-tier due to dead code, missing rank progression, and fundamentally visual nature — but no longer disqualified by silence.

- **2026-07-11:** Particle Life went from 25→27/30 by adding rank progression (8 ranks: Observer→Archon) + cleaning dead code. Pattern confirmed for the 10th time: cumulative milestone progression is the universal Return:4→5 fix. The simulation-specific twist: three bonus types that enhance the shepherd mechanic — wider radius (+5%/rank), stronger force (+6%/rank), and delayed extinction threshold (+2 gen/rank). The extinction delay is the most impactful: at max rank (Archon), extinction checks don't trigger until generation 19 instead of 5, giving experienced players more time to use the shepherd to rescue their species. This rewards the player's accumulated skill (shepherd positioning) rather than just giving free stats. Dead code cleanup removed 7 variables (paused, speed/speedIdx/speeds/speedLabels, mutationCount, dominantType) and the broken speed footer hint — the "↑↓ Speed" prompt in extinct state was a lie since ↑↓ were never handled in that state. Lesson: dead code isn't just technical debt — broken footer hints actively confuse users. Still B-tier at 27/30 due to fundamentally visual nature (particles aren't distinguishable by ear) and destructive Enter behavior, but the rank system gives return motivation. Return:4→5.

- **2025-07-11:** Neck Stretch went from 26→27/30 by adding mic-reactive ambient soundscape + breathing detection during holds. The biggest weakness was ZERO mic integration on a mic-first platform. Fix: two-oscillator drone (A2 sine + E3 triangle, perfect fifth) through a lowpass filter whose cutoff modulates with room noise level (200Hz in silence → 2kHz in noisy room). This creates environmental awareness — the same stretch feels warmer in a quiet room, brighter in a café. Breathing detection uses mic volume trend derivative (smoothed 0.06 lerp on delta) to track inhale/exhale transitions. During holds (the most meditative phase), steady breathing rewards with a harmony tone (A3 triangle, perfect fifth above drone) that fades in proportionally to sync quality. This closes the biofeedback loop: stretch + breathe steady = richer audio. Breath cycles counted on complete screen. ←→ toggles soundscape on/off (persisted) — previously ←→ were dead during exercise. D-pad:3→4. Audio:3→4. Pattern learned: mic integration as ambient accompaniment is valuable but not transformative. Compare to Stalk (mic IS the stealth mechanic, Audio:5) vs Neck Stretch (mic is ambient accompaniment, Audio:4). To cross into A-tier, the mic would need to be more central — e.g., mic-guided breathing as a formal phase between stretches, or ambient noise level affecting hold difficulty. Still, the improvement removes the disqualifying "ZERO mic" weakness and makes the experience feel more glasses-native. High B-tier, closer to A than before.

- **2026-07-11:** Neck Stretch went from 27→29/30 by making mic-guided breathing a core mechanic during holds. The previous lesson noted "mic as ambient accompaniment is valuable but not transformative" — this proves the fix: make mic data affect gameplay progression, not just ambience. The breathing guide shows an expanding/contracting visual ring + harmony tone (220→330Hz inhale, 330→220Hz exhale) during every hold phase. Mic volume trend derivative detects actual breathing and compares against guide phase. Good sync = hold timer advances up to 35% faster (breathGuideMatch × 0.35 bonus). Bad sync = normal timer speed (no penalty, just lost bonus). Three breathing paces (Slow 10s cycle / Medium 8s / Fast 6s) via ↑↓ — which were previously completely idle during exercise. Complete screen shows breath sync % with grade (S/A/B/C/D), creating competitive self-improvement. Pattern: the difference between "mic as decoration" (Audio:4) and "mic as mechanic" (Audio:5) is whether mic data changes a number the player cares about. In Stalk, mic changes detection radius. In Trader, mic changes volatility. Now in Neck Stretch, mic changes hold timer speed. The player cares about hold timer speed because it determines session duration. The breathing guide + harmony tone through bone conduction creates a "close your eyes and breathe" play pattern that's uniquely glasses-native — you don't need the visual at all during holds. D-pad:4→5 (↑↓ now active), Audio:4→5 (harmony tracks guide, not just steadiness), Hook:4→5 (sync grade adds competitive element). Promoted from B to A.

- **2026-07-11:** Particle Life went from 27→28/30 by replacing destructive Enter-to-reset with a Shepherd Pulse ability. The critical insight: Enter was the WORST button in the roster — a single accidental press during gameplay generated entirely new rules, resetting generation to 1 and wiping the run. No confirmation, no undo. The fix: Enter now triggers a Shepherd Pulse — a 0.75-second burst of 3.5× attraction force with 1.6× radius, on a 3-second cooldown. This transforms Enter from "game-breaking accident" to "strategic active ability." The Pulse creates a pumping rhythm: position cursor → pulse → reposition → wait for cooldown → pulse again. Visual feedback is layered: shockwave ring expands outward on activation, outer ring brightens and thickens during pulse, center dot enlarges, cooldown arc shows recharge, ready glow indicates ability available. Audio: rising triangle whoosh (0.5× → 1.5× species freq) + resonant sine ping at species frequency — satisfying through bone conduction. Rank bonuses that already scale shepherd radius and force now also scale the Pulse (3.5× of boosted values), making high-rank Pulses feel powerful. Pattern: if any button has a destructive action during the main gameplay loop, it's a UX bug, not a feature. Destructive actions belong in menus, with confirmation, or behind hold-to-confirm. Active abilities with cooldowns are the universal fix for "what should Enter do during gameplay?" — they provide a decision point (when to fire) and a rhythm (wait for cooldown). Hook:5 confirmed (pulse timing adds skill expression). Promoted from B to A.

- **2026-07-11:** Dungeon Crawl went from 29→30/30 by adding persistent rank progression (8 ranks: Novice→Dungeon Lord). Pattern confirmed for the 11th time: cumulative milestone progression is the universal Return:4→5 fix. The roguelike-specific twist: five bonus types covering all three survival pillars — HP and heal multiplier for sustainability, ATK and DEF for combat, and sight radius for exploration. The sight bonus is the most interesting because it's multiplicative with the existing spatial audio system — seeing further means hearing further enemies while also having more map revealed.
