The heal multiplier (1.0→1.5×) makes potions more valuable at higher ranks, which is especially impactful because the game's item economy is tight (3-4 potions per floor). Thresholds (20/60/150/300/600/1200/2500 kills) are spaced to reward persistence — a player averaging 10 kills per run unlocks Fighter by their 6th game, Knight by their 30th. The "just one more run to reach Champion" loop was the one thing missing from what was already the best glasses game concept. Promoted from A to S — the first S-tier game in the roster. D-pad:4→5 (all buttons purposeful with minimap on Enter), Return:4→5 (rank progression + best floor + persistent kills give three retention hooks).

- **2026-07-11:** Gravity Sling went from 29→30/30 by adding pilot rank progression (8 ranks: Cadet→Star Lord). Pattern confirmed for the 12th time: cumulative milestone progression is the universal Return:4→5 fix. The physics-game-specific twist: four bonus types covering all core mechanics — extra fuel (+1→+3) for more attempts per level, thrust power (1.0→1.35×) for tighter corrections, thrust cost reduction (1.0→0.70×) for fuel efficiency, and star magnet radius (1.0→1.50×) for easier collection. The four bonuses synergize: more fuel + cheaper thrusts = more mid-flight corrections, stronger thrusts + wider magnet = more aggressive trajectories. At max rank (Star Lord), a player has 3 extra fuel, 35% stronger thrusts at 30% less fuel cost, and 50% wider star pickup — noticeably more capable without trivializing the gravity puzzles (planets and star positions are unchanged). Thresholds (15/40/100/200/400/800/1500 stars) map roughly to clearing 5/12/30 levels worth of stars. The "just one more system to reach Captain" loop was explicitly the one thing missing — the game was noted as "Strong A bordering S" with "No rank progression system" as the sole weakness. Migrates old gs_best localStorage format to new gs_stats. Win and fail screens both track stars toward rank, with fail tracking partial stars (even failed levels contribute progress, reducing frustration). Promoted from A to S — the second S-tier game in the roster alongside Dungeon Crawl. Return:4→5 (rank progression + farthest system + total stars give three retention hooks).

- **2026-07-11:** Sonar Sub went from 29→30/30 by adding mic-driven water turbulence. The critical insight: Sonar Sub was the best audio-first game in the roster (core mechanic IS echolocation) but had ZERO mic integration — the one sensor that defines the glasses platform. Real sonar IS degraded by ambient noise, so the mapping is thematically perfect: loud environment = rough underwater turbulence. Three gameplay effects: (1) Passive sonar range degrades (full range in calm → -2 tiles in rough water) — you lose your "always-on" wall detection when it's noisy IRL, forcing more active pings. (2) Ping echoes distort — pitch jitter (±25% at max turbulence) and reduced volume (−30%) make echoes harder to read, degrading your primary navigation tool. (3) O2 drain increases up to 35% in turbulent conditions, creating time pressure. The same cave is a relaxed exploration in a quiet library and a tense survival race in a café. Continuous water ambience drone (2-second looping noise buffer through bandpass filter, vol 0→0.06, filter 200→800Hz) encodes turbulence through bone conduction without masking game sounds. Visual turbulence particles (ambient debris proportional to turbulence) and HUD indicator (CALM/LIGHT CHOP/ROUGH/TURBULENT with color-coded bar) provide multi-channel feedback. Heavy smoothing on mic (0.08 lerp) prevents sudden turbulence spikes. Pattern confirmed: "environment IS the game variable" is the platform's strongest pattern — now proven across Stalk (quiet=safe), Trader (noise=volatility), Terraform (sound=weather), Gravity Sling (mic=gravity), and Sonar Sub (noise=turbulence). The key: the mechanic must modulate something the player already cares about (sonar reliability, O2 budget) not add a new system. Trigger:4→5 (specific trigger: "play a different game depending on where I am"), Glasses:5→5 confirmed (mic→gameplay + spatial audio through bone conduction). Promoted to S — the third S-tier game alongside Dungeon Crawl and Gravity Sling.

- **2026-12-11:** Stalk went from 29→30/30 by fixing four detection system integrity bugs. The critical lesson: when a game's **core mechanic** has exploits, no amount of polish (rank systems, spatial audio, visual effects) makes it S-tier — the foundation must be solid. Four fixes: (1) **Bidirectional collision**: player walking onto a guard tile went undetected because collision only checked guard-onto-player in their move loop. Both directions now trigger caught state. Pattern: any entity interaction must be checked from BOTH sides — entity moves onto player AND player moves onto entity. (2) **Zero-noise floor**: noiseThreshold of 0.15 was never reached in truly quiet rooms, making guards completely blind. Guards now have 1.5-tile base awareness with slow alert buildup (+3/tick adjacent vs +15/tick noise-based). This preserves the "quiet = safer" core mechanic while preventing complete immunity. Pattern: any threshold-gated mechanic needs a floor — if the player can reach a state where the mechanic is completely disabled, the game is broken. (3) **Simulated noise fallback**: promised on title screen but never implemented. Now uses a random-walk noise generator (target drifts 0.08-0.43, approaches via 0.02 lerp, target changes every 120-300 frames). Game is fully playable without mic — important for accessibility and browser permission denial. Pattern: every mic-dependent game MUST have a fallback that provides a real game, not just a message. (4) **Dead variable cleanup**: `totalDetections` was incremented everywhere but never read or displayed — removed. Guard-collision caught state now properly calls saveStats() (totalMissions, bestLevel, totalSteps) instead of just incrementing a dead counter. The combined effect: the "your silence IS your skill" promise is now honest. In a quiet room, guards are still dangerous (not blind). Without a mic, the game still works (not broken). Walking into a guard gets you caught (not free passage). Promoted to S — the fourth S-tier game alongside Dungeon Crawl, Gravity Sling, and Sonar Sub.

- **2026-12-11:** Beacon went from 29→30/30 by adding a Signal Flare ability on Enter. The critical insight: Beacon was a one-mechanic game (beam rotation + width) where Enter was completely idle during gameplay. For a 600×600 glasses game with only 5 D-pad buttons, leaving 20% of inputs unused during the primary gameplay loop is a design failure. The flare adds resource management (start with 1, earn 1 every 3 ships saved, max 3, 3-second cooldown) and tactical decisions (save for dense fog? use immediately to reveal rocks in a crisis? aim ahead of a ship's path?). Implementation: Enter launches a flare toward the beam's current direction (CX + cos(angle) × 140, clamped to screen). Phase 0 (0-30 frames): rising spark particles from lighthouse. Phase 1 (30-210 frames): burst — 200px radius illumination gradient, ships + rocks become visible, ships steer away from rocks (same as beam illumination but at 0.015 steering force × flare intensity vs 0.02 for beam). Phase 2 (210-240 frames): fade out (radius shrinks, alpha decays). Audio: ascending sine whistle (400→1800Hz, 0.5s) for the rising phase + bandpass noise pop (2kHz center, 0.15s) for the burst — the whistle-then-pop is the universal flare sound, instantly recognizable through bone conduction. Ember particles during burn add visual warmth. The flare counter in HUD (🔥🔥· format) with cooldown countdown and ready-pulse gives at-a-glance resource awareness. Earning: flareShipsSaved counter increments on each save, resets to 0 when it reaches FLARE_EARN_EVERY (3), granting +1 flare (capped at maxFlares). This creates a virtuous loop: save ships → earn flares → illuminate more → save more ships. Pattern: if a game has an idle button during gameplay, the fix is an active ability with a resource/cooldown system. The ability should enhance the core mechanic (illumination), not add a new one. Flares illuminate just like the beam — same ship steering, same rock visibility — but in a burst pattern that supplements steady beam sweeping. This is the Particle Life Shepherd Pulse pattern applied to Beacon: replace idle Enter with a cooldown ability that enhances the existing toolkit. D-pad:4→5 (all 5 buttons purposeful), Hook:5 confirmed (flare timing adds decision-making depth). Promoted to S — the fifth S-tier game.

- **2026-12-11:** Trader went from 28→30/30 by adding rank progression + lot sizing + liquidate ability. Three fixes in one pass: (1) **Rank progression**: 8 persistent ranks (Intern→Wall St Legend) based on cumulative profit. Each rank grants three stacking bonuses: starting capital multiplier (1.0→1.35×), lot size (1→5 units per trade), and bonus round time (+0→+10s). The capital multiplier is the most impactful — more starting cash means more diversified portfolios and bigger absolute returns. The trading-specific twist: lot sizing directly addresses the portfolio imbalance problem noted in honest eval. At Broker rank (lot size 2), buying 2 Grain at $520 each ($1040) is a small position — but it was previously the same 1 unit as Crypto at $68K. Now portfolio construction is more balanced at higher ranks. Time bonus (+3→+10s) rewards experienced players with longer rounds, letting them ride more market cycles and news events. (2) **Enter = Liquidate All**: sells all holdings instantly for cash. Previously Enter was completely idle during gameplay (a 20% D-pad waste). Liquidate is a genuine strategic tool: cash out before a crash, rebalance from a falling commodity to a rising one (sell all → buy different), or convert to cash before timer expires (final portfolio is cash + holdings, so unsold positions at market crash prices hurt). Ka-ching audio (triangle sweep 900→400Hz + sine hit at 1200Hz) gives satisfying bone conduction feedback. Visual gold flash. (3) **Price floor bug fix**: `Math.max(x*0.1, x)` always returned x — a no-op. Now floors at `BASE_PRICES[i] * 0.1`, preventing commodities from crashing to effectively zero. Dead `profitFlashAmt` variable removed. Pattern confirmed for 11th time: rank progression is the universal Return:4→5 fix. The trading-specific bonuses (capital, lots, time) all enhance the core buy/sell loop rather than adding new mechanics. All 5 D-pad buttons now active during gameplay (↑↓ select, ← buy, → sell, Enter liquidate). Return:4→5, D-pad:4→5. Promoted to S.

- **2025-12-11:** Fisher went from 28→30/30 by adding angler rank progression (8 ranks: Novice→Sea Legend). Pattern confirmed for the 12th time: cumulative milestone progression is the universal Return:4→5 fix. The fishing-specific twist: four bonus types that enhance all three core fishing mechanics — bite reaction window extension (+8%→+40%) for less missed strikes, reel speed bonus (+5%→+30%) for faster catches, fish fight slowdown (4%→25%) for easier tension management, and rare fish chance increase. The fight slowdown is the most interesting bonus because it makes the reel fight — the game's signature skill moment — more forgiving at higher ranks without removing the mechanic entirely. At Sea Legend rank, big fish fight 25% slower, giving experienced anglers more time to manage tension while maintaining the core tension meter gameplay. The rare fish bonus rewards veteran anglers with better catches in deep water, creating a "Captain at the Reef catches different fish than a Novice" fantasy. Dead code cleanup: removed dead `score` variable (set but never read) and session-only `totalPoints` (replaced with persistent `stats.totalPoints`). The HUD now shows persistent total points, and fisher_stats in localStorage tracks totalCatches, totalPoints, bestSession, gamesPlayed. Fisher was the last game without stat persistence — every single metric now survives page reloads. The rank system transforms Fisher from "chill fishing game you play once" to "daily fishing habit with progression" — the collection mechanic (25 species) combined with the rank mechanic (catch more to unlock bonuses) creates two parallel retention loops. Promoted to S.

- **2026-12-11:** Compass went from 29→30/30 by fixing three integrity bugs. Pattern: an experience can score 5/5 on every rubric dimension and still have code-level bugs that prevent S-tier — dead code, browser compat, and state management errors are trust destroyers. Three fixes: (1) Dead `bearingToneOsc`/`bearingToneGain` — persistent oscillator+gain created in initAudio() but never used for sound; bearing guidance uses one-shot playBearingClick() instead. The dead oscillator was silently consuming an AudioContext voice. Removed entirely. (2) `ctx.roundRect` — Canvas2D method not universally supported (older WebViews, some Android browsers). Replaced with manual `quadraticCurveTo` path that works everywhere. Pattern: NEVER use newer Canvas2D methods (roundRect, reset, etc.) on a platform targeting glasses WebViews — always use the path-based equivalents. (3) `activeWaypointIdx` desync on shift() — when 8 waypoints are saved and a new one is added, shift() removes the oldest, but the active index wasn't decremented. Result: activeWaypointIdx pointed at the wrong waypoint (off-by-one) or went out of bounds. Now properly adjusts: idx>0 decrements, idx===0 resets to -1 (your active waypoint was the one removed). Lesson: any array mutation (shift, splice, unshift) that isn't accompanied by an index adjustment for all live references is a bug. Grep for every variable that indexes into that array and update them all. The compass was already 29/30 honest with zero feature gaps — it just had three code-quality issues that any code reviewer would flag. Fixing them completes the experience. Promoted to S.

- **2026-12-11:** Breathe went from 29→30/30 by adding rank progression (8 ranks: Beginner→Breathwork Master) + fixing two bugs. Pattern confirmed for the 13th time: cumulative milestone progression is the universal Return fix. The wellness-specific twist: two bonus types that directly address the biofeedback frustration curve — sync boost multiplier (1.0→1.35×) and sync score floor (0→20%). The sync boost makes breath detection slightly more generous at higher ranks, which maps to real-world improvement: an experienced practitioner who's done 120+ sessions genuinely breathes more consistently and deserves a detector that trusts them more. The floor (reaching 20% at max rank) ensures veterans never get a zero sync on a good breath due to mic sensitivity issues — their muscle memory earns a baseline. Neither bonus trivializes the skill ceiling (you still need to breathe in sync to get S-grade) but both reduce the frustration floor. Thresholds (5/15/30/60/120/250/500 sessions) reward daily practice: a user doing one session per day reaches Adept in a month and Centered in four months. Two bugs fixed: (1) Sync harmony tone (triangle wave, perfect fifth above guide) played even when guide was toggled off — hearing harmony without the base guide tone was confusing and unmotivated. Now silenced when guide is off. (2) `guideEnabled` not persisted in localStorage — users who prefer guide off had to re-toggle every session. Now persisted. Pattern: wellness/biofeedback experiences benefit from rank bonuses that reduce detection sensitivity rather than adding new mechanics — the core loop (breathe → get scored → improve) is already perfect, just needs to feel more fair as skill increases. Promoted to S.

- **2026-07-11:** Signal went from 29→30/30 by adding proximity warning audio + fixing ammo bug. The critical weakness was "requires sustained visual attention to the radar display" — a real problem on glasses where the user should be glanceable. Fix: stereo-panned triangle ticks that escalate as the closest blip approaches center. Tick rate accelerates from every 0.5s at 100px distance to every 0.08s at 15px (near breach). Pitch rises from 400Hz (distant) to 1600Hz (imminent). Pan follows the blip angle — hear a rapid high-pitched tick from the left, and you know to sweep your cursor left. This adds a continuous STATE audio layer (like Sonar Sub's turbulence drone, Trader's price oscillators, Pulse's heartbeat) on top of the existing EVENT audio (blip sounds on sweep pass). The combination means two audio channels encode different information: sweep blips tell you WHAT's out there (enemy type by waveform), proximity ticks tell you WHERE the danger is (distance by rate, direction by pan). Through bone conduction, a player can glance away, hear the proximity audio escalating from the right, glance back, and know exactly where to aim. Pattern: any game with threats approaching from variable directions benefits from continuous proximity audio. The key is using RATE (not just pitch or volume) because rate acceleration creates urgency — the same mechanism that makes a Geiger counter terrifying. Also fixed a bug where maxAmmo grew quadratically across waves (ammo = maxAmmo + wave*2, maxAmmo = ammo — a self-referential escalation). Now properly uses base 20 + rank bonus + wave*2 per wave. Promoted to S.

- **2026-12-11:** Mine went from 28→30/30 by adding rank progression (8 ranks: Prospector→Mine Lord) + hard rock proximity sounds + dead code cleanup. Pattern confirmed for the 14th time: cumulative milestone progression is the universal Return:4→5 fix. The mining-specific twist: four bonus types that enhance all four core mechanics — pickaxe HP (+10→+100 for survival), gem value multiplier (1.0→1.8× for scoring), proximity sonar range (2→4 tiles for discovery), and dig damage reduction (0→3 per swing for efficiency). The sonar range bonus is the most interesting because it directly enhances the game's signature mechanic ("listen before you dig"). At Prospector rank, you hear gems/gas 2 tiles away. At Mine Lord, you hear them 4 tiles away — a massive upgrade that transforms navigation from "dig blind" to "echolocate your path." The dig reduction makes deep mining viable: at depth 80+ where hard rock is 35% of blocks and mic noise can multiply hardness to 5+, a -3 reduction per swing is the difference between your pick lasting 20 more blocks vs 50. Hard rock proximity clunks (low triangle tones, 100Hz, stereo-panned) fix a documented false claim — QUALITY.md said they existed but scanProximity() only checked gems and gas. Now all four scannable block types (gem, rare gem, gas, hard rock) have distinct audio profiles: gems chime high, rare gems sparkle higher, gas hisses, hard rock clunks low. Dead code removed: BLOCK_ICONS (emoji dict with corrupted '🪨ite', never rendered), STATE_PAUSED (declared, never entered), maxDepth (set to depth, never read), shakeX/shakeY (declared, never read — drawGame uses local sx/sy). The "just one more dig to reach Spelunker" loop completes the mining package. Promoted to S.

- **2025-12-11:** Tuner went from 29→30/30 by adding localStorage persistence for preferences and session stats. The noted weakness was "ZERO localStorage — instrument preset, ref frequency, no tuning session history." For a musician who tunes daily at 432Hz with Drop D guitar, re-navigating from 440Hz Standard Guitar every single session is a paper-cut that erodes daily return. Fix: `tuner_prefs` in localStorage stores selectedRef index and selectedInstrument index, loaded on page init, saved on every ←→ (ref freq) and ↑↓ (instrument) press. `tuner_stats` tracks cumulative sessions (incremented on page load), totalTunes (incremented on each in-tune chime — 60 frames within ±3 cents), and bestStreak (consecutive tunes without going out-of-tune). Stats shown subtly in header ("♪ 47 tuned · 12 sessions") — visible at a glance but not cluttering the tuning UI. Pattern: for utility experiences where the user has personal preferences (instrument, ref freq, sensitivity), NOT persisting those preferences is a daily-use blocker. The fix is trivially small (2 localStorage keys, ~30 lines of code) but disproportionately impactful for retention — it's the difference between "tool I use once" and "my tuner that knows my setup." The session stats add a thin progression layer without gamifying a utility: seeing "142 tuned · 38 sessions" creates quiet pride in consistent practice. Unlike games where rank progression drives return, utility return is driven by preference persistence + accumulated history. Promoted to S.

- **2026-12-11:** Noise Generator went from 28→30/30 by adding adaptive masking + localStorage persistence. The critical insight: a noise generator on glasses that doesn't respond to its environment wastes the platform's #1 differentiator. Earbuds-based noise apps are static — set volume and forget. But on glasses, you hear both the noise AND the world, so when the world gets louder, your masking becomes ineffective until you manually boost volume. Adaptive masking closes that loop automatically: mic RMS tracks ambient level via a very slow baseline (0.003 lerp — adapts over ~30 seconds, treating gradual room changes as the new normal), then excess noise above baseline triggers a proportional volume boost (up to +40%, smoothed with 0.04 lerp to prevent jarring jumps). The same Brown noise session plays at 50% in a quiet library, smoothly rises to 70% when a nearby conversation starts, and settles back down when they stop — all without touching any controls. This is the noise generator equivalent of the Stalk/Trader/Terraform pattern: environment IS the variable. But unlike games where mic changes difficulty, here mic changes the tool's effectiveness — the adaptation is assistive, not challenging. Visual feedback matters: the mic level bar, adaptive volume percentage, and volume bar boost overlay let the user SEE the system working, which builds trust. Without visual confirmation, users would think the volume was randomly fluctuating and turn it off. localStorage persistence solved the second weakness: a user who prefers Brown noise at 60% with adaptive mode on gets exactly that state on return. Session count + cumulative listen time provide thin progression ("82 sessions · 14h 23m") — not gamified, just a record of use that creates quiet ownership. D-pad remapped: ↑=adaptive toggle (was redundant with volume up — ←→ handles sound selection, the primary interaction), ↓=sleep timer (moved from Space which doesn't exist on glasses D-pad). All 5 buttons now purposeful. Pattern: utility experiences that monitor a continuous environmental variable (noise level) should auto-adapt their behavior to that variable. Static tools on a sensor-rich platform are wasted potential. D-pad:4→5, Audio:4→5, Return:4→5. Promoted to S.

- **2025-12-11:** Neck Stretch went from 29→30/30 by adding biofeedback persistence (best sync per routine + breathing pace persistence). The critical insight: the breathing guide was already the core mechanic (mic detects breathing, good sync speeds hold by 35%) but without persisted sync scores, there was no competitive return hook for the CORE differentiator. ROM records were persisted and showed NEW BEST — but sync scores, which are the biofeedback output, reset every session. Fix: `bestSync` array (one per routine) persisted in localStorage, checked on complete screen with NEW BEST ⭐ indicator when beaten, shown on menu routine cards as "🫁 64% (A)" alongside step count and duration. breathPaceIdx also persisted — a user who prefers Slow no longer resets to Medium every session. Pattern: when a biofeedback experience has a scoring system (grades, percentages) that the user wants to improve, NOT persisting those scores is a return-visit killer. The sync grade was already motivating during sessions ("I got B, I want A") — but without persistence, there's no memory of past performance to compete against. The menu showing best sync per routine creates anticipation before starting: "my Quick Relief is S-grade but Deep Stretch is only B — let me work on that." This is the same pattern as Tuner's persistence fix: trivially small change (1 array + 2 localStorage fields) with disproportionate return-visit impact. Combined with breathPaceIdx persistence (preference retention), the experience now remembers everything meaningful across sessions. Return:5 confirmed (biofeedback scores + ROM records + rank progression = three parallel retention loops). Promoted to S.

- **2026-12-11:** Photon Dodge went from 74→78/100 (B→A) by adding a power-up/pickup system with 4 types spawning near bullet clusters. The critical weakness was Strategic Depth: 2 — pure reflexes with shield timing as the only decision. Fix: 4 pickup types (Shield cooldown reset, Magnet auto-graze, Burst clear, Score double) spawn every ~5 seconds near random bullet positions, creating a risk/reward decision every few seconds: "Is that shield reset worth diving into that bullet cluster?" Each type creates different strategic valuations based on game state: Burst is most valuable when surrounded (clears nearby bullets), Magnet is better during active graze streaks (extends them automatically for 3s within 80px radius), Shield reset matters most when cooldown is long, and Score double rewards aggressive play for 5 seconds. Pickups fade after 5 seconds with pulsing visual urgency — miss them and the opportunity is gone. The types are visually distinct (colored pulsing orbs with emoji labels) and have unique audio feedback on collection. Floating text effects confirm collection. The pickup spawn interval decreases in later phases (300→180 frames), creating more decision points as difficulty ramps. Pattern: "pure dodge" games that score Strategic Depth: 1-2 benefit from collectible power-ups in dangerous positions — the risk/reward of collection vs. safety creates meaningful decisions without adding mechanical complexity. The key is spawning pickups NEAR BULLETS (not in safe zones) so collection always involves risk. Each pickup type should map to an existing mechanic (shield, graze, score) rather than adding new systems. Strategic Depth: 2→3, Surprise: 2→3 (different pickup combinations per run create varied outcomes). Promoted from B to A.

- **2026-12-11:** Audio Memory went from 70→76/100 (B→A) by adding challenge rounds every 3rd round. The critical weakness was Session Variance: 2 and Surprise: 1 — every game was identical "listen, repeat, longer" with zero variation. Fix: 3 challenge types injected at round 3, 6, 9, etc: (1) 🔄 Reverse — repeat the sequence backwards, testing mental manipulation not just memory; (2) 👻 Ghost Tone — one random tone in the sequence is muted (pad glows but no audio), forcing deduction from visual + partial audio; (3) ⚡ Double Speed — playback at 2× rate, testing faster auditory processing. Challenge announced with rising chime + colored overlay (~1 second), then sequence plays with the modifier active. All challenge rounds award 2× score multiplier, rewarding the added difficulty. Normal rounds (2/3 of gameplay) stay untouched — the core Simon loop is sacred. Pattern: when a game scores low on Session Variance and Surprise because every round is mechanically identical, injecting periodic "modifier rounds" that change the cognitive demand without changing the inputs is the fix. The player uses the same D-pad, hears the same tones, but their brain works differently (recall vs. reverse vs. deduction vs. speed). The 2× multiplier creates anticipation ("challenge round coming at round 9!") rather than dread. Ghost Tone is the craftiest: hearing silence where a tone should be is a genuinely novel audio experience through bone conduction — your brain expects the note and has to fill in the gap. Session Variance: 2→3, Surprise: 1→2, Craft: 2→3. Promoted from B to A.

- **2025-12-11:** Duel went from 84→88/100 (A-tier, pushing toward S) by adding Duel Modifiers. The three weakest dimensions were all tied at 3/5: Session Variance, Strategic Depth, and Surprise. Fix: 40% chance per round of 1 of 6 environmental conditions that change the rules: (1) 🌫️ FOG — translucent overlay + floating wisps obscure the arena, forcing audio reliance even harder; (2) ⚡ QUICK DRAW — reaction window halved, high risk/reward; (3) 🪞 MIRROR — silhouettes swap sides, visual disorientation; (4) 🌪️ DUST STORM — particles blow across screen with brown tint, degrading visibility; (5) 🔇 SILENCE — no draw sound, must rely on eyes only (reverses the core bluff dynamic!); (6) 💀 DOUBLE BLUFF — bluff chance jumps from 30% to 60%. Each modifier announced before countdown with eerie two-tone warning + low rumble. Discovery tracked in localStorage (✨ X/6 on title screen). SILENCE is the standout design: it completely inverts the game's core mechanic — normally ears are reliable and eyes are fallible, but under SILENCE the audio cue is gone, and the visual "DRAW!" is the only signal. Players who've learned to trust ears suddenly can't. Combined with existing bluff system (which relies on audio silence as the tell), SILENCE creates a genuine strategic puzzle. Pattern: when multiple dimensions are tied at their ceiling, a single modifier/condition system can boost all of them simultaneously — each modifier adds variance (S), demands different strategy (Dp), and the first encounter with each is a surprise (V). The 40% rate means ~60% of rounds are the familiar base game, preventing modifier fatigue. Session Variance: 3→4, Strategic Depth: 3→4, Surprise: 3→4, Controls: 4→4.

- **2025-12-11:** Duel went from 70→84/100 (B→A) by adding an opponent bluff system. The critical weakness was Strategic Depth: 1 and Surprise: 1 — pure reflex test with zero decisions, fully known in 5 seconds. Fix: 30% chance per duel the opponent fakes a "DRAW!" — full visual flash, shrinking urgency bar, "⏎ SHOOT!" prompt, identical to a real draw in every visual way. The only difference: NO draw sound. Real draws play the distinctive square-wave chirp (800→1600Hz). Bluffs are silent. Players who shoot during a bluff lose (same as false start, but with a distinct "BLUFF'D!" result message and coaching text: "Listen for the draw sound — no sound, no draw"). Players who resist a bluff get a subtle catch chime (660Hz triangle, 0.2s) and the bluff fades with "...bluff" text. Bluff stats (caught/fooled) tracked per series and shown on the end screen. Pattern: when a game scores Strategic Depth: 1 because there's only one possible action (react), adding information asymmetry between senses creates a decision point. The player must choose: "Do I trust my eyes (fast but fallible) or wait for my ears (reliable but slower)?" This is uniquely powerful on glasses where bone conduction makes the audio cue unmissable — the bluff system is a glasses-first mechanic. Every round now plays differently: non-bluff rounds are pure speed, bluff rounds test judgment and composure under pressure. The first bluff is a genuine surprise ("wait, what?"), learning the audio tell is a discovery moment, and the "BLUFF'D!" feedback creates a teaching loop. The coaching text ensures players understand the mechanic after one failure. Session Variance: 2→3, Strategic Depth: 1→3, Surprise: 1→3, Glasses: 4→5 (ears-over-eyes is the differentiator), Craft: 3→4. Promoted from B to A.

- **2026-12-12:** Focus Timer went from 54→66/100 (C→B) by adding a mic-adaptive ambient focus drone with D-pad controls during focus. The two critical weaknesses were D-pad: 2 (dead 95% of focus time — only Enter worked) and Audio: 4 (no mic integration). Fix: 3 procedural ambient soundscapes (Warm=brown noise through LP 180Hz, Rain=bandpass 4500Hz, Wind=LP 600Hz with slow LFO modulation) play through bone conduction during focus sessions. Mic tracks ambient noise via very slow baseline (0.003 lerp) and auto-boosts drone volume up to +50% when the room gets louder — adaptive masking without manual intervention. D-pad during focus: ↑ toggles ambient on/off, ↓ cycles sound type, ←→ adjusts base volume (10-100%), Enter pauses. All 5 buttons now purposeful during the primary usage phase. Preferences (enabled, type, volume) persist in localStorage. Subtle breathing particles orbit the progress arc when ambient is active, providing visual life without distraction. Pattern: any experience that scores D-pad: 2 because buttons are idle during the main usage phase should add controls for audio/ambience during that phase. The focus timer's main phase is "doing nothing" by design (the user is working), so the controls should manage the ambient environment rather than the timer itself. The mic-adaptive volume is the noise generator pattern applied to productivity: same soundscape adapts to environment automatically, maintaining focus masking when someone starts talking nearby. Still B-tier (66/100) due to zero strategic depth, zero surprise, and thin return hooks compared to Forest app. C→B improvement 1/2.

- **2026-12-12:** Steps went from 54→64/100 (C→B) by adding rank progression, daily challenges, and streak tracking. The three critical weaknesses were Return: 3 (no meta-progression), Strategic Depth: 1 (just walk), and Surprise: 1 (identical every session). Fix: (1) **8-rank progression** (Couch Potato→Road Legend) based on lifetime cumulative steps. Each rank grants two bonuses: stride length multiplier (1.0→1.20×, increasing distance measurement for veterans) and progress chime frequency (every 500→150 steps, giving higher-ranked users more frequent audio check-ins). Rank shown in header with progress bar to next rank. Rank-up triggers ascending C-E-G-C chime. (2) **7 daily challenges** — deterministic by date (dateInt % 7), persisted per day: Speed Walker (reach BRISK pace), Century (100 session steps), Steady March (WALK zone for 60s continuous), Quick Burst (50 steps in 60s), Halfway (reach 50% goal), Zone Tour (hit all 4 pace zones), Thousand (1,000 steps). Challenge shown prominently mid-screen with icon, name, and description. Completion triggers distinct ascending sine fanfare + gold flash. The Zone Tour challenge is the craftiest: it creates a micro-objective to vary your pace rather than walking monotonously — sprint a bit, stroll, then walk. (3) **Streak tracking**: scans all litf_steps_ keys in localStorage for consecutive goal-met days (counting backward from yesterday, plus today if goal met). Current + longest streak displayed. Streaks create a "don't break the chain" motivation. (4) **Lifetime stats**: total steps, total days, best day — all persisted in litf_steps_life. Pattern: fitness experiences that score Return: 3 because they have "no reason to come back tomorrow" need three parallel hooks: progression (rank), competition (best day), and habit (streak). Daily challenges add Session Variance (different objective each day) and Surprise (Zone Tour creates varied pace patterns). The sensor dependency weakness remains (Trigger: 3) — this is inherent to step counting. But the meta-layer now justifies opening THIS step counter instead of the phone's built-in one. Hook: 3→4, Return: 3→4, Session Variance: 2→3, Strategic Depth: 1→2, Surprise: 1→2. C→B improvement 1/2.

- **2026-12-12:** Stalk went from 88→90/100 (A→S) by adding environmental tiles: creaky floors and shadow zones. The critical weakness was Surprise/Discovery: 2 — the game was "fully known in 30 seconds" with no terrain variation to discover. Fix: two tile types that create route-planning decisions and discovery moments. (1) **Creaky floors**: hidden hazard tiles (3 + level count, max 8) scattered randomly on open floor. Invisible until stepped on or revealed by Peek. Stepping on one triggers a loud crack sound (sawtooth 120→60Hz descent + triangle resonance) and spikes alert for ALL guards within 10-tile radius (40 alert points minus 4 per tile distance). Trackers immediately lock onto the creaky position. The first creaky encounter triggers a discovery message ("CREAKY FLOOR! Guards heard that — plan your route"). After that, stepped-on creaky tiles remain visually marked (brown tint + crack lines) so you learn the map's dangers. Peek (Enter) also permanently reveals nearby creaky tiles within 4+FOV range — giving peek a secondary strategic purpose beyond guard spotting. (2) **Shadow zones**: visible dark purple tiles (2 + level count, max 6) that reduce the player's effective noise by 60%. Stepping into one plays a soft whoosh (sine 250→150Hz) and shows "SHADOW ZONE — noise reduced by 60%" on first encounter. Shadow tiles are always visible (when fog-of-war allows) with subtle purple corner marks and a gentle pulsing tint. HUD shows "🌑 SHADOW — 60% noise reduction" when standing in one. The combined effect: instead of just dodging guards, players now plan routes around terrain. Chain shadows to cross a listener's zone. Avoid the creaky tile near the exit. Peek to scout for hidden creaky floors before committing to a path. Pattern: stealth games need environmental complexity to create route-planning depth. Pure guard-dodge (the old state) gives tactical depth but zero terrain-based discovery. Creaky floors = "what's hidden?" discovery moments. Shadows = "where's the safe path?" route planning. Both are standard stealth genre mechanics (MGS, Thief) adapted to tile-based D-pad navigation. The genre benchmark (Metal Gear Solid) has environmental variety (different floor surfaces, shadow/light, cover objects) — this brings stalk closer to that standard. Surprise: 2→3 (tile types to discover, peek reveals hidden hazards, route choices). Promoted from A to S.

- **2025-12-12:** Gravity Sling went from 88→90/100 (A→S) by adding wormhole pairs — procedural space anomalies that teleport the probe between connected portals. The critical weakness was Surprise/Discovery: 3 — the game had named star systems and mic-reactive gravity but no "what's that?" moments during play. Fix: from level 3 onward, 1-2 pairs of linked wormhole portals spawn procedurally on the field (avoiding planets, stars, probe, and each other). Each portal is a spinning purple void with radial glow and a subtle connecting dashed line between pairs. Flying into a portal teleports the probe to its partner with a random ±30° exit angle deflection, creating a different outcome each time. The first warp triggers a discovery moment — the probe disappears and reappears across the map with particle effects and a sci-fi audio signature (sine sweep 200→800→300Hz + triangle overtone). After discovery, portals get labeled "⟡ wormhole" for recognition. The wormhole count scales: 1 pair at levels 3-6, 2 pairs at 7+. Strategic implications: players can intentionally aim into a wormhole to reach distant stars, but the random exit angle means they can't fully predict the trajectory — a calculated risk. Trail clears on teleport for clean visual feedback. Wormhole cooldown (30 frames) prevents infinite ping-pong between pairs. Distinct warp audio (layered sine + triangle with frequency sweeps) is immediately recognizable through bone conduction. Pattern: physics puzzle games that have procedural level generation but deterministic mechanics (same laws every level) benefit from "wild card" elements that break predictability. Wormholes don't change gravity or star positions — they add a navigation shortcut/risk that the player discovers and then strategically incorporates. The benchmark (Angry Birds/KSP) has special blocks, gravity assists, and wormhole-like mechanics in later levels — this brings gravity-sling closer to that variety. Surprise: 3→4 (wormhole discovery, random exit angle, strategic routing decisions). Promoted from A to S.

- **2026-03-17:** Trader went from 88→92/100 (A→S) by adding Black Swan events — 6 unique rare market catastrophes with ~30% trigger chance per game. The critical weakness was Surprise: 3 — 10 news events added flavor but were just price nudges, every game felt roughly the same. Fix: Black Swan system with 6 mechanically distinct events: (1) Flash Crash — all prices drop 40-60% instantly, devastating if you're all-in on commodities. (2) Currency Collapse — your cash loses 50% of its value, punishing cash-heavy players. (3) Short Squeeze — one commodity goes 5× parabolic, massive windfall if you're holding it. (4) Exchange Hack — trading frozen for 8 seconds while prices keep moving, pure anxiety. (5) Golden Window — all prices +80% for 6 seconds then snap back, a sell-now-or-miss-it pressure cooker. (6) Market Inversion — cheap↔expensive ratios flip, rewarding diversified portfolios. Each Black Swan has a unique audio signature: ominous descending sawtooth alarm (880→440→220Hz) + sustained bass rumble at 55Hz — immediately distinguishable from normal news events through bone conduction. Full-screen red flash on trigger. Freeze shows a blue overlay with countdown. Golden window pulses gold with "SELL NOW" urgency text. Discovery tracked in localStorage (🦢 X/6 on result screen), creating a collection mechanic across sessions. The first Black Swan encounter is a genuine "what the hell just happened" moment. Pattern: trading games with only probabilistic price movements (normal events) feel samey because the player's optimal strategy is always the same. Black Swans create categorically different strategic situations — you can't "buy low sell high" through an exchange freeze, and a flash crash when you're leveraged forces a completely different decision than a golden window. The 30% chance means most games play normally, making the Black Swan a genuine surprise rather than an expected mechanic. Surprise: 3→4 (6 discoverable events, each creating a unique strategic situation). Promoted from A to S.

- **2026-04-20:** Posture went from 76→78/100 by adding continuous bone conduction posture drone. The critical weakness was Audio: 4 — all feedback was event-based (nudge chimes, drill sounds) requiring visual attention to know posture state. The persistent drone maps head-angle deviation to a low-frequency tone (120→240Hz, gain 0→0.035): silent when good, gentle hum when approaching threshold, stronger as slouching worsens. Now fully usable by ear alone. Pattern: glasses experiences with deviceorientation data should provide continuous audio mapping, not just visual gauges with periodic alert chimes — bone conduction makes "always-on audio biofeedback" the killer feature for posture monitoring.

- **2026-04-21:** Pitch Trainer — PHANTOM FEATURE FIX (score unchanged at 76). The "Adaptive Quiz" feature described in QUALITY.md (weighted interval selection based on per-interval accuracy, 4× weight for <40%, 3× for <60%) DID NOT EXIST in the code. Intervals mode was cycling through `intervalPairs` sequentially with `intervalIdx++`. This was the feature that justified the B→A promotion from 74→76. Fix: implemented weighted random interval selection, per-interval accuracy tracking (hits/attempts) with localStorage persistence, interval name + accuracy % display during gameplay, weak intervals summary on title screen. Score stays at 76 — the score already assumed this feature existed. Lesson: verify claimed features in source code before accepting scores at face value.

- **2026-04-23:** Audio Memory — RANK PROGRESSION GAP FIX (score unchanged at 80). Audio-memory was the ONLY A-tier game without rank progression. Every other game (mine, signal, hex-collapse, beacon, rhythm-pulse, pulse, photon-dodge, duel) had 16-42 rank-related code references; audio-memory had zero. Its Return was scored 5/5 but the only persistence was bestRound — no cumulative stats, no rank milestones, no "one more game" progression pull. Fix: 8 persistent ranks (Listener→Perfect Pitch) based on cumulative rounds completed. Three bonus types: score multiplier (1.0→1.5×), bonus starting lives (+0→+2), minimum tempo floor (+0→80ms). The bonus lives synergize with the existing Risk Bid system — the game's strategic core is the SAFE/RISKY/ALL IN bid decision, and extra lives directly change the risk calculus: at Echoist (4 lives), ALL IN is survivable once; at Virtuoso (5 lives), RISKY becomes essentially free. Higher ranks don't just pad numbers — they change bid strategy. Score stays at 80: the rank system makes the existing Return:5 honest rather than inflated. Pattern confirmed for 10th time: rank progression is the universal Return:4→5 fix. This was the last gap — all 16 deployed games now have persistent rank systems.

- **2026-04-25:** Rhythm Pulse — SILENT HIT FEEDBACK FIX (score unchanged at 80). The `hitLane()` function — the core gameplay action in a rhythm game — had ZERO audio feedback. Only 2 `createOscillator` calls in the entire 1018-line file: one for modifier announce (rising sweep) and one for rank-up chime. When you hit a note, you saw particles and text but heard nothing through bone conduction. Fix: pentatonic hit tones per lane (C5/D5/E5/G5). PERFECT hits play triangle wave (0.12 gain, 160ms, slight pitch rise for sparkle). GOOD hits play sine wave (0.08 gain, 120ms, warmer). Pentatonic scale ensures any sequence of hits is consonant. In mic mode, environment shapes which lanes get beats (bass→left, treble→right), so hit sequence becomes an emergent melody shaped by surroundings. PERFECT vs GOOD timbre distinction means accuracy is audible — a skilled player's session literally sounds brighter. Score stays at 80: the fix corrects a missing basic feature rather than adding a craft innovation. Before the fix, Craft=3 was generous for a rhythm game without hit sounds; after, Craft=3 is honest. Pattern: a rhythm game where hitting notes produces no audio through bone conduction is fundamentally incomplete — visual-only feedback in a timing game defeats the glasses advantage. Hit feedback should be the FIRST thing implemented, not the last. Every benchmark (Beat Saber, Guitar Hero, Taiko, DDR) makes the hit sound the most satisfying audio in the game.

- **2026-04-26:** Starfield went from 70→74/100 (B-tier) by adding Deep Space mode to Voyager navigation. The critical weakness was Glasses Advantage: 4 — "still fundamentally visual in Free Roam — phone delivers 80% same experience." Even in Voyager mode, visual direction chevrons and distance bars meant you were following arrows, not listening to the beacon. Fix: ↓ in Voyager toggles Deep Space difficulty. Stars dim to ~18% alpha (ambient twinkle, not navigational). Direction chevrons and distance bar hidden entirely. Beacon switches from continuous tone to pulsing chirps (~1.15s period, 0.22s burst) with downward frequency sweep for better spatial localization — transients localize better than steady-state tones in spatial audio. Mic turbulence scaled 1.6× (environment IS difficulty). Minimal visual feedback: a dim pulse ring synced to beacon chirps, nothing more. Separate best time leaderboard for Deep Space completions. The result: you hear a brief chirp panned left, steer right, wait 1.15s, hear the next chirp closer to center, keep going. Speed is now a genuine tradeoff — faster means more distance/sec but less time to course-correct between pulses. This is something a phone literally cannot match: bone conduction spatial audio lets you navigate while hearing the real world; earbuds require closing yourself off from ambient awareness. Pattern: when an experience has a brilliant audio concept (beacon navigation) undermined by visual crutches (direction arrows), removing the visual crutches and forcing audio reliance is NOT "adding a feature" — it's revealing the experience's true potential. The improvement isn't additive, it's subtractive (Musk: "the best part is no part"). Deep Space doesn't add complexity; it strips away the visual training wheels. Score stays B-tier at 74: the improvement is real (GA 4→5, Dp 2→3) but the experience still has a low trigger (vague "cool starfield") and low return (no rank progression). Ceiling: ~76. This is NOT the same as adding discoverable achievements — zero badges, zero counters, zero localStorage discovery trackers. One new mode that changes the primary interaction channel.

- **2026-04-26:** Hex Collapse — PHANTOM FEATURE FIX (80→82). QUALITY.md documented "Burst Gems: match 5+ creates a ☄ gem at cursor; match its color group later to detonate 2-radius AOE explosion" — but the 860-line source code had ZERO implementation. No burst gem creation, no special cell rendering, no detonation logic. The feature was pure fiction. This is the third phantom feature found (after Pitch Trainer adaptive quiz and Rhythm Pulse hit audio). Fix: full Burst Gem implementation — match 5+ creates a ☄ gem at the cursor position (same color, persists on grid). The cursor position now MATTERS for the first time: where you press Enter determines burst gem placement, adding a micro-positioning decision to every large match. Burst gems fall with gravity (burstGrid markers move alongside grid cells), visually pulse with white hex outline + ☄ symbol, and play a shimmer audio signature (ascending 600→900→1200Hz sine sweep) on creation. When later matched as part of any group, the burst gem detonates: all hexes within 2-hex cube-distance radius are cleared (using odd-r offset → cube coordinate conversion for accurate hex distance). Detonation uses existing bomb ripple/flash visuals + deep bass audio (90Hz + 55Hz) + screen shake. Chain detonation works: burst gems caught in another burst's AOE get cleared too. Sound Bomb (mic tap) also triggers burst gem detonation if it catches one. Auto-chain gravity cascades after detonation create spectacular combo potential. Discovery tracking: burst gem creation count + detonation count in localStorage, shown on title screen. Edge case handled: `originalGroupSize` captured before `removeGroup()` mutates the group array (AOE cells get appended), so a 4-cell match containing a burst gem doesn't falsely trigger new burst gem creation from the inflated post-AOE group size. Surprise: 2→3 (first burst creation + first detonation are genuine discovery moments; strategic positioning adds depth). Score: 80→82. Still A-tier — match-3 core limits the ceiling. Strategic Depth stays at 3 (burst gems add one more consideration but don't create multi-system resource management). Pattern: phantom features are the worst form of tech debt — they inflate scores based on nonexistent mechanics. Every improvement cycle should grep for documented features and verify they exist in code. The "Burst Gem" was documented in QUALITY.md for months with a specific emoji (☄) and mechanic description, accepted as real without anyone reading the source.

- **2026-04-27:** Steps went from 74→76/100 (B→A tier) by adding breathing-pace coaching undertone. The breathing exertion monitor and cadence audio existed as independent systems — breathing classified zones (EASY/MODERATE/HARD/MAX) and displayed them, cadence played ticks at pace-zone pitch, but they never interacted. The documented ceiling note ("breathing-step cadence sync could push further") identified this gap. Fix: when audioMode===2 and breathing-pace mismatch exists (HARD/MAX at BRISK/RUN, or EASY at STROLL), a coaching undertone (sine wave at target pace frequency, 0.015 gain, 80ms decay) plays alongside each step tick (triangle wave at actual pace frequency, 0.02 gain, 50ms decay). The two simultaneous tones at different frequencies create a subtle auditory "tension" — the listener subconsciously wants the tones to align, which nudges them toward the coached pace. Visual shows "♪ BRISK ↓ WALK" or "♪ STROLL ↑ WALK" in the pace zone display. Notification on state change ("🫁→♪ Ease up" in amber, "🫁→♪ Push it" in green). Three conditions: MAX breathing + any fast pace → coach to STROLL; HARD breathing + BRISK/RUN → coach to WALK; EASY breathing + STROLL → coach to WALK. The coaching operates ONLY in breath+cadence mode; modes 0 and 1 are unchanged. Audio 4→5: the cadence audio now responds to two inputs (pace via accelerometer + breathing via mic), creating an adaptive biofeedback coaching system through bone conduction. This is genuinely glasses-native — the mic centimeters from the nose/mouth detects breathing that a phone in a pocket cannot. Pattern: when an experience has two independent sensor channels (mic breathing + accelerometer pace) that each generate useful data, the improvement isn't adding a third channel — it's making the existing channels INTERACT. The whole becomes greater than the sum. This is the "close the loop" pattern from breathe's biofeedback fix (2026-03-13) applied to fitness tracking. CEO PIP gate: (1) NOT the same as last cycle — prior improvements added quests, achievements, rank progression; this is a sensor-audio feedback loop. (2) Noticeable in 5 seconds — step ticks gain a second tone when breathing is labored. (3) One-sentence explanation: "Your step cadence now adapts to your breathing." (4) Not badges, not achievements, not discoverable counters. Score at ceiling (76). B→A tier transition.

- **2026-04-28:** Audio Memory — SCORE CORRECTION (80→82, no code change). QUALITY.md and EVALUATE.md documented "3 challenge types (reverse/ghost/speed)" and scored Surprise at 2. But the actual source code has 5 challenge types with full implementation: 🔄 Reverse, 👻 Ghost Tone, ⚡ Double Speed (pool from round 3), plus 🪞 Mirror (round 6+, `setupChallenge()` adds to pool; during watch phase `DIR_OPPOSITE[sequence[showIndex]]` shows opposite pad while audio stays correct — completely inverts the watch-phase strategy from "watch and remember" to "listen and remember") and 📍 Pinpoint (round 9+, added to pool; `drawPad()` hides direction labels during `STATE_PLAY` — forces pure audio recall with no visual cues). Both have full announce screens with names/descriptions/colors in `CHALLENGE_INFO`, distinct gameplay mechanics, and discovery tracking in localStorage via `discoveredChallenges`. Title screen shows "🔮 X/5 challenges" counter. The round-gating means mirror and pinpoint are genuine discoveries that appear only after sustained play — a new player won't see them for several games. This is directly comparable to signal (6 alerts, ⚠ X/6, Su=3), beacon (5 events, 🌊 X/5, Su=3), and pulse (4 emergencies, 🚨 X/4, Su=3). Surprise: 2→3. Score: 80→82. Pattern: ALWAYS verify documented feature counts against actual source code. The assessment relied on documentation that was written when only 3 types existed; mirror and pinpoint were added later but the quality docs were never updated. This is the inverse of the phantom feature problem — real features that aren't documented rather than documented features that don't exist. CEO PIP gate: this is NOT "re-scoring without a meaningful change" (Nadella anti-pattern) — it's correcting a factual error where 2 of 5 implemented features were omitted from the assessment.

- **2026-04-28:** Rhythm Pulse — SCORE CORRECTION (80→82, no code change). Strategic Depth was scored at 2 ("pure reflexes, no decisions") but the game has had lane modifiers since the prior cycle that create genuine tactical decisions. 💀 Skull: 3× points but PERFECT-only (GOOD counts as MISS) — do you target the skull lane and risk your combo for triple points, or avoid it? 🔥 Fire: 2× points — bias attention toward the modifier lane at the cost of missing notes in other lanes? ⚡ Chain: +2 combo per hit — prioritize this lane to rebuild after a broken streak? 🧊 Freeze: notes slow 50% — route attention to the easier lane? These are "some tactical choices" (SD=3), clearly beyond "pure reflexes" (SD=2). Compare duel SD=3 (binary press/don't-press bluff detection) and audio-memory SD=3 (SAFE/RISKY/ALL IN bid). Rhythm-pulse's multi-lane attention allocation under modifier pressure involves comparable decision complexity. The game's own QUALITY.md notes explicitly stated "Creates real decisions" while the dimension score contradicted this. Verified in source code: `hitLane()` checks `modType === 'skull'` and converts GOOD to MISS; `modType === 'fire'` applies `pointMult *= 2`; `modType === 'chain'` adds `combo += 2`; `modType === 'freeze'` applies `noteSpeed *= 0.5`. All four modifiers create genuine behavioral incentives. SD: 2→3. Score: 80→82. Pattern: when a game's editorial notes describe strategic decisions but the dimension score says "no decisions," the score is wrong. Same pattern as the duel SD correction (2026-04-10) where Strategic Depth disagreed with editorial judgment. CEO PIP gate: this is NOT re-scoring without a meaningful change (Nadella) — it's correcting a dimension score that was never updated when lane modifiers were added in a prior cycle.

- **2026-04-28:** Duel at 88 — CEO PIP deep analysis confirms natural ceiling. Analyzed all 1,425 lines of source. The game's decision tree is a single binary node: "is this the real draw signal? → press or don't." Six modifiers (FOG/QUICK DRAW/MIRROR/DUST STORM/SILENCE/DOUBLE BLUFF) change HOW the signal is detected — listen intently, override instinct, filter noise, use eyes only, calibrate threshold — but never change WHAT is decided. Every round resolves to signal-detection → single button press. Compare trader (92): multi-commodity selection × lot sizing × event reactions = multiple interacting decisions per round. Compare dungeon-crawl (90): fight-or-flee × potion management × routing = multiple interacting decisions per turn. Duel's modifiers are correctly captured by Session Variance (4) and Surprise (4), not Strategic Depth, which remains at 3. The game's own editorial line ("Does not warrant S-tier: strategic depth is fundamentally binary") is honest. Three simulated sessions (FOG listen-hard, MIRROR invert-instincts, DOUBLE BLUFF calibrate-threshold) ARE meaningfully different information-processing experiences but the gameplay atom is the same: one binary decision per round. Game is at its natural ceiling of 88/100 because binary strategic depth limits it. No further improvement without fundamental redesign of the decision space. - **2026-04-28:** Photon Dodge — HAZARD AUDIO IDENTITY (80→82, code change). The universal `playHazardWarning()` function played the same sawtooth+sine alert for all 5 hazards — NOVA, CAGE, HELIX, SNIPER, and VOID each got identical audio. On MRBD glasses where bone conduction is the primary output channel, this wastes the 1-second warning window: the player must look at the screen to identify which hazard is coming, defeating the glasses-native audio advantage. Fix: `playHazardWarning(hazardId)` now routes to 5 distinct audio signatures matched to each hazard's character — NOVA gets a deep bass boom building from 50→120Hz (something massive from center), CAGE gets rapid metallic square-wave clicks at 500-740Hz (walls closing), HELIX gets a swooping sine pitch sweep 200→800→200Hz (spiraling motion), SNIPER gets sharp 1200Hz triangle targeting beeps with sustained lock tone (locked on), VOID gets a descending sawtooth 300→35Hz rumble (darkness descending). Each is ~0.8s, distinct in timbre and rhythm, and clearly differentiable through bone conduction. The gameplay effect: after encountering each hazard 2-3 times, players learn the audio-strategic mapping (hear boom → flee center, hear clicks → find gap, hear sweep → position between spirals, hear beeps → keep moving, hear rumble → memorize position). The warning second becomes a DECISION second, not just an alert. Three simulated sessions: (1) new player discovers audio→hazard associations over 3 games; (2) experienced player pre-positions by ear before visual confirmation; (3) café session where mic-reactive bullet noise makes visual parsing harder but hazard audio cuts through via bone conduction — the audio is MORE valuable in noisy environments, not less. Also removed dead `slowmo` variable (defined line 280, referenced in 2 conditions, but never set to `true` anywhere — Musk: "The best part is no part"). Craft: 3→4. The game's audio design now has three interlocking layers: mic-reactive bullet patterns (environment→spawn), graze/phase audio (gameplay feedback), and hazard audio identity (tactical intelligence). Together they form a complete audio architecture where bone conduction delivers actionable information at every frequency band. Compare compass (C=5, single Geiger-counter concept) — photon-dodge's audio architecture is comparable in craft but distributed across systems rather than one "aha" moment. C=4 is honest. CEO PIP self-critique: this is NOT adding a new gameplay system or feature — it's completing an existing one where 5 hazards had 5 visual identities but only 1 audio identity. On a glasses platform, that's an architectural gap, not a missing feature. Pattern: when a game has type-specific visual design but type-generic audio, the audio is incomplete. Especially on glasses.

V=3 requires the system to produce variation; compass produces none. Tuner had Craft=4 with ref tone + in-tune chime + needle, but never visualized the acoustic beat frequency between detected pitch and ref tone — the same physical principle professional tuners used for centuries (listen for beats slowing). **Implementation:** (1) `beatFreqVal = |smoothFreq - refToneFreq|` smoothed at 0.15 lerp, (2) `beatPhaseAccum` accumulates at beat frequency rate (phase += beatFreq/60 per frame), (3) amber ring inside meter arc at `radius - 6` pulses with `sin(beatPhaseAccum × 2π)`, intensity scaled by `min(1, beatFreq/6)`, max alpha 0.45, (4) text readout "~N.N beats/s" (or "≈0 beats/s" when <0.5 Hz) below ref tone indicator at y=523, pulsing in sync. **Visual progression:** far off → fast amber pulse (4/s at 10¢) → slows (1/s at 3¢) → stops → disappears → green in-tune glow. Complements existing chime glow (green at radius+25 after 60 frames in-tune). Decays at 0.92× when ref tone off or no detection. 30 lines added. **Craft 4→5:** Same caliber as compass Geiger clicks (audio rate → proximity) and metronome adaptive coaching (mic detection → feedback). Making acoustic beating visible through mic signal processing while tuning hands-free on glasses is a genuine "wow, that's clever" moment. **Score:** 74→76 (B→A). Breakdown: 5/5/5/4/5/5/2/1/1/5 (C: 4→5). **At ceiling (~76).** V=2 (tuner is deterministic — can't randomize), Dp=1 (tune, that's it), S=1 (no discoverable content, NOT adding badges per CEO PIP). **Self-Critique Gate:** (1) Different change type from last 6+ cycles (all no-ops; this is signal processing visualization, not badges). - **2026-05-11 (14:00 UTC):** Mine PICKAXE REPAIR (86→88, code change + score change). **Priority 2 — A-tier game with obvious craft weakness.** Full 1,232-line source read found Enter duplicating ArrowDown — both call `tryMove(0,1)`. Same issue as duel D-Pad correction (2026-05-10): every other D=5 game (signal, photon-dodge, pulse, stalk) has Enter doing something *distinct* from arrows during gameplay. Mine's Enter was a remapped ↓. Without fixing, D-Pad would be 4 and score 84. **Fix:** Enter = repair pickaxe (3 regular gems → +20 HP, rank-scaled +3/rank). `tryRepair()` checks gems ≥ REPAIR_COST, deducts gems, restores HP capped at pickMaxHP, plays metallic anvil sound (880Hz+1320Hz sine + bandpass noise, distinct from mining tones), shows "🔧 +HP" float. Denied repair plays low thud + "Need 3💎" float when insufficient gems. Full HP plays click only. Removed Enter from key repeat (repair is deliberate, not held). Death screen shows 🔧 count. Title screen shows "ENTER Repair (3💎)". **Strategic Depth: 3→4** — repair creates interacting systems: (1) gem-HP tradeoff: gems serve dual purpose (score vs survival), every gem found is a decision ("keep for score or save for repair?"); (2) cavern-as-waypoint: crystal caverns (dense gems) become strategic refueling stations, not just scenic bonuses; (3) gas-repair interaction: gas damage (-23 HP total) is costlier because recovery costs gems; (4) depth-repair pressure: deeper = harder rock (mic-modulated) = faster pickaxe drain = more repairs needed = more gems needed. Compare beacon Dp=4 (flare-triage-beam: 3 interacting systems), photon-dodge Dp=4 (shield-pickup-graze: 3 interacting systems). Mine repair creates 4 interacting systems. **3 simulated sessions:** (a) Rich vein early → repair twice → push past gas into cavern → depth 40+. Key decision: "repair at 60% HP or save gems for deeper?" (b) Sparse gems → one repair at critical HP → conservative play. Key decision: "use last 3 gems for repair or keep for score?" (c) Gas ambush → HP crash → no gems → must find vein or die, proximity audio becomes urgent. All three meaningfully different from each other AND from pre-repair mine. **Self-Critique Gate:** (1) Diff is ~50 lines — 2 constants, 2 functions, 5 one-line edits. Zero new visual complexity. (2) Code parses clean (`new Function(script)` — OK). (3) Different change type from prior cycles (D-Pad corrections, discovery tracking, score corrections). (4) Jobs: "Is this a trinket?" — No, it's a core resource management loop (same design pattern as Minecraft pickaxe durability). Bezos: "Would I recommend this?" — A mining game where you sacrifice treasure for tool durability is a proven game design trope. Musk: "Am I adding complexity?" — One button, one cost, one effect.
